Antennas Propag., 2023, DOI 10.1109/TAP.2023.3287676 (explicit, semi-implicit and implicit lumped elements in 2D; a search summary says the semi-implicit inductor's stability does not depend on L; paywalled); Kung and Chuah, Progress In Electromagnetics Research, 2003, a paper proposing energy-based stability theorems for FDTD with lumped elements (open access at jpier.org, but its download page sat behind a human-verification gate, so it was not read; title, first names and DOI not verified); F. Bekmambetova, X. Zhang and P. Triverio, "A dissipation theory for three-dimensional FDTD with application to stability analysis and subgridding," arXiv:1705.02274 (related DOI 10.1109/TAP.2018.2869617; read to the end of its Section II only: it proves stability by dissipativity for materials and subgridding but does not treat lumped R, L or C, so it gives the method for an energy argument and no inductor criterion).
32. R. P. Brent, C. Percival and P. Zimmermann, "Error bounds on complex floating-point multiplication," Math. Comp. 76(259):1469-1481, 2007, DOI 10.1090/S0025-5718-07-01931-X (from search results; abstract only seen: the usual four-multiplication complex product has error at most sqrt(5) 2^-p relative to |z0||z1|; the three-multiplication (Karatsuba/Gauss) form is stable in norm but the smaller component can have a large relative error; not read in full).
33. A. Smoktunowicz and I. Wrobel, "Numerical aspects of computing the Goertzel algorithm," arXiv:math/0407177, 2004 (abstract only: Goertzel is much less accurate than the FFT; divide-and-conquer variants improve it; not read).
34. W. Kahan, "Further remarks on reducing truncation errors," Commun. ACM 8(1):40, 1965, DOI 10.1145/363707.363723, and A. Neumaier, "Rundungsfehleranalyse einiger Verfahren zur Summation endlicher Summen," ZAMM 54(1):39-51, 1974, DOI 10.1002/zamm.19740540106 (both DOIs from memory, not verified; neither read; cited for compensated summation, which the accumulator and the planned far-field sums use).
35. N. J. Higham, Accuracy and Stability of Numerical Algorithms, 2nd ed., SIAM, 2002 (textbook; not read here; search results attributing a particular complex-multiplication constant to it came from patent documents and were not checked).
Candidates not yet pinned down: non-uniform-grid FDTD papers giving practical grading-ratio limits; GPU FDTD implementation papers (to read, not to copy code from).

- 2026-11-19, **plugin works inside KiCAD 10.0.7 (macOS)**: after the icons were added and installed with `plugin/tools/install.sh`, the Simulate button appears (see `plugin/README.md` for the install steps and what was learned: toolbar buttons need icons, tracing with WXTRACE=KICAD_API, the user plugin path). Run from outside KiCAD against a live editor, the action exported the W3008 board (12 copper polygons), defined the port across J1 pads 1 and 2, and started the solver window.
- 2026-11-19, **patch mesh convergence on the GPU** (`out/convergence/`, `scripts/convergence.sh`; resonance of the best match, design 2.45 GHz, FR-4 er 4.5): cells per wavelength 20 / 30 / 45 give 2.113 / 2.220 / 2.236 GHz without grid snapping, and 2.187 / 2.203 GHz (20 / 30) with grid lines snapped onto copper edges. Snapping helps at the coarse end and not at 30. An earlier run with explicit 0.5 mm and 1 mm cells (about lambda/100 and lambda/50) put the dip at 2.4 GHz, so the model is still 8 to 10 percent low at the presets and the cause is **open**: candidates are the refinement box around the probe port (fine lines run across the whole patch), the vertical cell size (thickness/3), the thickness of the absorbing layer in cells, and a probe model that is too coarse. Until this is understood the Medium preset is not suitable for resonant antennas and the UI must not present it as accurate.

## Sample designs per profile (planned 2026-11-19)
Other profiles need other boards and ranges. Lesson from the W3008: a chip antenna has no published internal geometry, so it cannot be simulated; each sample board must have a **PCB-trace antenna with published dimensions**, with real supporting parts found through Octopart (radio IC or module, RF connector, matching passives). Candidates found (nothing generated yet; every dimension must be checked against the original document):
- **2.4 GHz (Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, Zigbee, LWMesh):** TI SWRA117 / DN007 / AN043 "2.4 GHz Inverted F Antenna" (published dimensions, tuned for 50 ohm at 2.45 GHz, strongly stackup dependent) and Silicon Labs AN1088 (inverted-F, notes that matching parts differ per implementation). Radio side: for example a TI CC26xx/CC2652 or Silicon Labs EFR32 part, via Octopart.
- **Sub-GHz (868/915 MHz LWMesh, Zigbee sub-GHz):** Octopart shows Linx ANT-868-SP / ANT-916 planar "Splatch" antennas, Abracon ACAJ-110-T (9 x 3 x 1.2 mm), TE ANT-868-HESM (helical), Pulse W3320 (868/915/2.4 GHz chip). All are components without public internal geometry, so a printed meander or monopole design from an application note is needed for the simulated sample; Atmel AT86RF212 is the LWMesh sub-GHz radio.
- **UWB (channels 5 and 9):** Taiyo Yuden AH086M555003 (surface-mount, channel 5; chip, no geometry), Qorvo DW3300Q transceiver (Octopart datasheet: channels 5 and 9 at 6489.6 and 7987.2 MHz). The simulated sample needs a printed UWB monopole from the open literature (for example the PMC papers on planar UWB antennas).
- **5 and 6 GHz Wi-Fi:** a printed IFA or patch from an application note; band edges are verified (U-NII-1/3, 5925-7125 MHz).

### Test matrix: chip antennas and PCB-trace antennas (user requirement, 2026-11-19)
Every profile gets at least one board of each kind, so the pipeline is not tuned to one antenna type:

| Profile | PCB-trace antenna (fully simulated, validated against its application note or an analytic result) | Chip antenna (board-level model only) |
|---|---|---|
| Wi-Fi / Bluetooth / Zigbee / LWMesh 2.4 GHz | rectangular patch (done, snapshot only); TI SWRA117 / DN007 inverted-F board (to generate in KiCAD) | Pulse W3008 test board (exists, `boards/w3008-testboard/`) |
| LWMesh / Zigbee sub-GHz | printed meander or monopole for 868/915 MHz (to find) | Abracon ACAJ-110-T or Pulse W3320 on a reference board |
| UWB ch 5 / ch 9 | printed UWB monopole from the open literature | Taiyo Yuden AH086M555003 |
| Wi-Fi 5 / 6 GHz | printed IFA or patch | (any 5 GHz chip antenna, optional) |

**What a chip-antenna result means** (to be stated in the run summary and the preview whenever the model contains an antenna part): the radiating structure is inside the ceramic body and is not public, so the part is modelled as a dielectric block (body from the footprint courtyard or 3D model, permittivity a user setting with a labelled default) between its real pads. The simulation then covers the feed line, ground plane, clearance and neighbouring parts, and shows how the board changes the match, but it does not reproduce the part's own resonance. Validation for these boards is therefore a before/after comparison (change the ground clearance or feed, see the shift) and a check against the datasheet's own S11 only as a plausibility check, never as a pass/fail. Required exporter work: component bodies (courtyard box, later 3D model) as dielectric blocks, selectable per footprint (docs/ux.md, model selection).

## Next steps (macOS only, to be refined)
Done so far (details in Measured so far): IPC probe and exporter; skeleton, bandwidth benchmark, GPU kernel with material palette; CPU reference and GPU with CPML and lumped ports; snapshot format (`docs/snapshot.md`), voxelizer with component selection, graded lateral grid, in-plane and vertical ports; S11 extraction and Touchstone; CPU-rendered slice animation (PNG frames and a window, `emfdtd --view`); first plugin entry point (`plugin/plugin.json`, `action_simulate.py`, untested inside KiCAD).
1. **GPU:** the tests (CPML, port, palette, engine) and the 0.5 mm patch run passed on 2026-11-19 after a 45 minute wait for the shared lock. Next: convergence (1.0, 0.5, 0.35 mm) of the patch resonance against the cavity-model prediction (2.45 GHz design, FDTD at 1 mm cells shows the dip near 2.4 GHz).
2. **Validation set beyond the patch:** quarter-wave monopole (resonance, about 36 ohm), a 50 ohm microstrip line (reflection and Z0 against the closed form), the CPWG feed of the reference board. The W3008 chip was thought not modellable (see Measured so far); a test of the vendor's outer metal geometry is set up but not run (see "Is the W3008's radiator just its visible Ag metallization?"); the reference board validates the feed, ground and clearance only.
3. **GPU DFT accumulators at display resolution and the volume view** (ray marcher, empty-space skipping) per Viewer design; the slice window currently reads fields on the host after each submit, which is adequate for planes. Add a second-slice and field-component choice, and a copper/geometry overlay for the volume.
4. **Grading policy:** refinement boxes are explicit or around ports today. Derive them from the geometry (small gaps and features), keep the smoothing ratio and the smallest cell visible in the run summary, estimate memory and time before the run and refuse with a message.
5. **Exporter gaps:** real board outlines (arcs, non-rectangular), arc tracks tested on a board that has them, footprint 3D models and component bodies (dielectric blocks), copper thickness and solder mask, unfilled zones handled without changing the board.
6. **Plugin:** test the action inside KiCAD (install into the plugins directory, run with a pad pair selected); then the UX/UI design with the user (port choice, component selection model/view, frequency range, results panel).
7. Later: Windows and Linux builds, CI, packaging and distribution (PCM vs direct builds), patent check before any conformal scheme.
    - **BDD gaps to close later (noted 2026-11-20 at the user's request; nothing started).** The user's rule is scenario first, tolerance in the THEN. What the session did not follow: (1) **the window has no scenarios**: legends, axis triad, layer toggles, Smith chart, sweep, probe, model-selection and body panels were checked by screenshot and accessibility dump only, so the legend and triad click bug reached the user; nothing ever clicked. Plan: a headless UI test target that drives `ui::Ui` with the existing `--ui-click` machinery (`pressAt`, `releaseMouse`, `frame`) and reads the accessibility tree and state, with scenarios written first for each control (a legend header click collapses and reopens it; each triad end sets the camera pose of `viz::stdViewPose`; the layer checkboxes change what is drawn; the S11 view buttons switch views; the pin keeps and clears a reference; the sweep panel refuses a bad key or value; the model section's combos change the selection and the sidecar file). (2) **Several scenarios were written with or after the code**, and some tolerances were set from the first measurement (the DFT bounds, capacitor and inductor accuracy, the far-field balance, the net walk, sweep and geometry-override tests, the voxelizer's bodies): from here on write the scenario, see it fail, then the code, then the measured tolerance with the measurement beside it. (3) **The GPU sweep scenario is hidden** (`[.sweepgpu]`) and never runs in `scripts/check.sh`; give it a quicker model or a release-built GPU label so it runs. (4) Scenarios for the remaining untested paths: the Far field tab's numbers against a synthetic far field, the VTK/OBJ export from the window, the probe panel's run wiring, `--part` and `--nets` parsing.
    - **Proposals for four open items (2026-11-20, researched with the advisor; for the user to choose, nothing built).**
      1. **Ground nets in the window: a role per net, not a separate list.** Each net row in "Parts and nets" gets a combo: Auto (shows the guess and its evidence, "by name GND" or "covers 31 % of F.Cu"), Signal (the walk starts here), Ground (kept as the reference, never walked), Left out. Un-grounding a false positive (the patch's own RF net is labelled "looks like ground or a plane" today) is one click. Saved both ways in `selection.json` (`extraGroundNets` exists; add `notGroundNets`), with a "Reset to guessed" button and an accessibility name "Role of net RF". Matches the conventions found: Sigrity lets a user mark a net as power or ground from a right-click, Altium's PDN Analyzer lists the DC nets for confirmation, AWR's setup wizard propagates a net selection and skips power and ground nets and can carry it across series parts ([Cadence AWR note](https://resources.system-analysis.cadence.com/i/1355107/1), [Sigrity forum](https://community.cadence.com/cadence_technology_forums/system-analysis/f/sigrity/62349/how-to-create-power-nets-gnd-nets-and-vrms-for-the-pcb-design-for-sigrity-power-dc-simulation), [Altium PDN Analyzer](https://altium.com/documentation/altium-nexus/pdn-analyzer-example-guide-v2)). Alternative rejected: a plain \"ground nets\" text box (nothing shows why a net was guessed). KiCAD's API has `get_netclass_for_nets` (seen in the installed kipy; not tried against a live KiCAD): the exporter could send each net's class name as a third hint (a class called GND or Power), unverified.
      2. **3D pattern surface: a layer in the existing 3D view.** The usual form is a surface whose radius follows the gain and whose colour follows it too ([MATLAB tool description](https://www.mathworks.com/matlabcentral/fileexchange/71973-plot-3d-antenna-radiation-patterns), [TU Berlin notes](https://commit-web.commit.tu-berlin.de/en/book/sci-python/chapter/ch17/section/s03): cuts alone hide sidelobes and nulls). Ours: radius proportional to gain in dB over a 30 dB floor, drawn from the existing theta x phi grid (36 x 72, about 5 000 triangles), scaled to about half the display box and drawn through the 3D tab's camera and axis triad as a \"pattern\" layer over the board (the \"far field on the CAD\" idea cited above), coloured by the chosen colour map (CC0 data already shipped), legend \"gain, dBi\". Triangles sorted back to front each frame (cheap at this size) and drawn translucent. The Far field tab keeps the cuts and numbers (the accessible form) and gets a \"View in 3D\" button. Export: OBJ (writer exists) and VTK polydata. No dependency. Do not read AntennaVis or TkAnt (GPL); the sources above were read for behaviour only. Alternatives rejected: a separate pattern window (loses the board context), a spinning thumbnail (not accessible).
      3. **Strip-resolved S11 for wide ports: change what is shown, not the solver.** Algebra checked (2026-11-20): the mean strip voltage equals Vs - z0 times the total current, i.e. the Thevenin terminal voltage of the parallel strip generators, so |S11| < 1 on a lossless load is not a definition error: power circulates between strips through the source resistors, and (1 - |S11|^2) counts it as loss in the generator. Proposal: (a) headline magnitude |S11|_P = sqrt(1 - P_acc / P_avail) from `fdtd::acceptedPower` (already built; 1.000 on the lossless pad, 1/3 on the 100 ohm load), phase from the mean-voltage S11; (b) per-strip reflection coefficients G_k = 2 V_k / Vs - 1 drawn as a shaded band on the dB plot and a fan on the Smith chart, with the strip spread in the run notes: the strip-resolved view, and it explains the existing note; (c) a one-click \"narrow this port to a third of the copper\" in the preview, never silent. Later and separate: a modal wave port (the conformal-FDTD waveport literature, [abstract only seen](https://bvbvf1.bib-bvb.de/vufind/PrimoRecord/cdi_ieee_primary_7506276); not read). Alternative rejected: weighting strips by current to force |S11| <= 1 (hides the spread, and the result depends on the load).
      4. **Refinement boxes from geometry (Next steps 4).** Rules from documented behaviour, no code read: cells per wavelength (have), a setting `cells_per_feature` (default 3) for the smallest feature, smoothing ratio at most 1.3 (have), minimum cells through a dielectric (have). The openEMS documentation recommends a mesh line one third inside and two thirds outside a metal edge to tame the edge singularity, spacing of metal lines of at most lambda/20 and of substrate lines lambda/10 as examples, and neighbouring cells within about a factor 1.5 ([openEMS wiki, FDTD Mesh](https://wiki.openems.de/index.php/FDTD_Mesh.html), [concepts page](https://openems.readthedocs.io/en/latest/concepts/mesh.html); documentation read for behaviour only, the code is GPL and stays unread; the wiki's wording on \"smallest feature\" sizing was not found, so the rule above is ours). Detect from the snapshot: gaps between copper of different nets (part gaps, port gaps), the narrowest dimension of each copper shape, via diameters. Emit a refine box per feature with cell = size / N and a reason. UX: an Advanced-tier list under \"Before you run\" (\"gap R1 pads 0.4 mm -> 0.13 mm cells, +0.3 M cells\") with an on/off per box and the estimate updating, drawn in the \"model as the solver sees it\" view; refuse a run below one cell across the smallest feature with a message that names the feature. Alternatives rejected: one global fine cell (cost grows with the whole board), silent automatic refinement (violates the project rule that every default shows its source).
    - **What needs the user or their machines (cannot be done in this session):** run the plugin inside KiCAD with a board that has real pads and check the exporter's pads, DNP flags and footprint boxes (only a fake board has tested them); try VoiceOver and the keyboard on the new controls by hand; Windows and Linux builds and the GPU box; GitHub CI and a release; the PCM question; a reference solver or a published patch for the absolute patch S11; whether to commit and push the work since 127f25e (the user commits).

**Standalone window, theme and accessibility (built 2026-11-19, macOS; details in `docs/ux.md`).** Own CPU ImGui rasteriser presented through a Vulkan swapchain with GLFW. Theme Auto/Light/Dark with configurable field colours (`data/ui_defaults.json`), pan and zoom in the slice, board-outline toggle, system accessibility preferences (Reduce Motion, Increase Contrast, Reduce Transparency, text size) read on macOS (Windows and GNOME readers written, untested). Screen readers: every widget records a node in a platform-neutral tree (`src/viz/access.hpp`); AccessKit (`src/viz/screen_reader.cpp`, behind `FDTD_SCREEN_READER`, on only on macOS) exposes it to VoiceOver. Keyboard navigation is on (arrows, Space/Enter, Tab in text fields). Checked: tree rules in `viz_test`, `--ui-a11y-dump` (no unnamed control on any tab), live read of the tree through the macOS accessibility API. **Not checked:** the press-toggles-control fix after the click-hold change, VoiceOver itself (needs a person: Cmd+F5), the keyboard keys by hand, Windows and Linux adapters (one branch in `screen_reader.cpp`: `accesskit_windows_subclassing_adapter_new`, `accesskit_unix_adapter_new`, with the option on).
8. **Accessibility follow-ups:** try VoiceOver and the keyboard by hand; Focus-request support (moving the screen reader cursor moves keyboard focus); slider increment/decrement actions; pan, zoom and rotate of the slice and volume from the keyboard; a visible focus ring everywhere; points of the plots navigable one by one; a scalable bundled font (the built-in one is coarse at 200 %); Windows and Linux adapters with the text-size and preference readers exercised there.
9. **Versioning, CI and releases (built 2026-11-19, not yet run on GitHub).** Semver in `VERSION`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `scripts/version.sh`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (macOS arm64 build with `-Werror` and sanitizers, CPU tests, clang-format; Python 3.9 lint and tests) and `release.yml` (tag `vX.Y.Z` -> release build, CPU tests, TGZ plus SHA-256, smoke test of the unpacked archive, GitHub release with the changelog notes). Verified locally: the release build, `emfdtd --version`, the archive unpacked elsewhere running with the source `data/` folder moved away, the version checks. **Not verified:** the workflows themselves (YAML parses only), whether `brew install shaderc vulkan-headers vulkan-loader molten-vk` gives CMake its `glslc` and loader on the runner, the sanitizer build on the runner. Still open: code signing and notarisation, bundling the Vulkan loader and MoltenVK (the archive needs Homebrew's), Windows and Linux archives, PCM packaging, the AccessKit licence files shipping in `share/doc` (installed; check the list when a release is cut).
10. **PCB properties as settings (built 2026-11-20).** `data/fab_profiles.json` now carries each profile's dielectric stack; `app::applyPcbSettings` (`src/app/prepare.cpp`) applies it to a copy of the snapshot's stackup before voxelizing. Tests in `settings_test.cpp`. Open: the PCBWay 6-layer and larger stackups (ask PCBWay or read the page in a browser); the datasheet of the laminate PCBWay actually uses for Df (its page lists several); OSH Park's "temporary alternate stackup for some 4 layer orders" (changelog 28-JAN-2026; the note text and the stackup images were not readable); the KiCAD plugin does not yet pass the board's finish and copper weight, so the copper settings only record.
11. **Visual features and parametrics (decided 2026-11-20; background in "How other tools show and drive EM simulation").** One item at a time, each with its BDD scenario (tolerance in the THEN) written first, `scripts/check.sh` green, and a line in "Measured so far" before the next starts. Order: (a) the GPU phasor accumulator; (b) surface current on copper; (c) phase animation and a Poynting slice; (d) the colour-map table and View setting; (e) Smith chart, VSWR and impedance; (f) near-to-far-field transform, pattern view, gain, directivity, radiated power and efficiency [28]; (g) sweeps: registry-key sweep driver with overlaid S11, then the geometry-override sweep; (h) VTK image-data export, threshold isosurface, probes. The remaining small items (freeze-and-compare, voxel-model toggle) follow.
    - **Status 2026-11-20:** (a) done; (b) done (computation, run wiring, drawing in the 3D tab and the slice tab, layer toggles); the 3D axis triad done; component bodies done as a global setting. Next is item 12, then (c).
12. **Components in the model (decided 2026-11-20, order agreed with the user).** (a) **Lumped R, L, C between a part's two pads**, starting with the 0 ohm link and the resistor (a conductance on the edges across the gap, no solver change), then the capacitor, then the inductor (a current-state update in the solver: needs a cited method and a scenario against the analytic impedance). Values come from the part's value field when it parses (0R, 2.2nH, 1pF), else a user entry. The run summary must say that two-terminal parts are not modelled until this exists (it does not yet). (b) **Net-driven selection**: the user picks nets (not single traces), and parts are included automatically:
    - **Status 2026-11-20 (end of the autonomous pass):** (1) to (6) done and (7) done in part: the window has the per-part treatment list, the per-component body list (Default, Ignore, Dielectric with permittivity, Metal), bodies drawn in the preview, the net picker and the sidecar file; not done: a way to add ground nets in the window, lumped parts with series resistance or parasitics, an S-parameter block for a module. Item 11 (a) to (h) and the small items are built and measured (see Measured so far). Item 13: the baselines and known-value tests are in; the remaining items are listed in its last measured line.
    - every part with a pad on a picked net is included; from a two-terminal part the net on its other side is added and the walk continues, so a feed line reaches the antenna through its matching network;
    - the walk stops at ICs, connectors, modules and any part with three or more pads (the user can add them, or they become a labelled black box once bodies are used);
    - ground and plane nets never start a walk (nearly every part touches ground); ground is included as the reference, and a nearby ground pour is kept so the return path is not lost;
    - a hop through a part is followed only if that part can be modelled, so (b) comes after (a);
    - today tracks and zones are always in the model; net selection drops copper on unselected nets, with a note;
    - the result is an editable list with the reason per part ("pad on net RF_FEED") and a one-click remove; the preview highlights what is in and out; the run summary lists each net and part with its reason; the choice is saved in the sidecar file, never in the KiCAD files. (c) The per-part body treatment list in the window and the preview drawing of bodies. Open: whether kipy 0.8.0 exposes the active design variant for a board (the installed package has `variant` only in schematic symbol and job types, none in the board footprint type, so assume no).
    - **Decided after research and the advisor (2026-11-20).** **Pads are exported, not inferred.** The snapshot gets an optional `pads: [{number, net, x, y, layer}]` per component (same pattern as `body`, no version bump), read from the footprint's pads (kipy `Pad` has `number`, `net`, `type`, `position`; they sit in the footprint definition's `items`; the accessor is unverified against a live KiCAD session, like `get_item_bounding_box`). Inferring from `copper` shapes with `owner == ref` is fragile: through-hole pads appear once per layer, custom pads are several polygons, two pads on the same net are indistinguishable by geometry, and thermal-relief copper has `owner ""`. A part is two-terminal when it has exactly 2 pads on 2 different nets. The reference prefix (R, L, C, FB, JP) is only a hint for the value parser, never the classifier; diodes (D) are never lumped (nonlinear). The pad positions give the gap a to b directly, so a lumped element reuses the port plan (a strip of edges across the gap, `refineAroundPorts`) without a source.
    - **Jumper marked DNP.** The board file has the flag: kipy's `FootprintAttributes.do_not_populate` (also `exclude_from_bill_of_materials` and `mounting_style`, SMD or through-hole; DNP is a KiCAD 8 or later attribute, so snapshot.md states the floor). The exporter writes `attributes: {dnp, mounting}`. Meaning: **DNP is not fitted, so open** (pads stay copper, nothing spans the gap), the default and what the board says. Whether a DNP part "may be fitted" is a variant question, and the board API does not appear to expose variants, so the answer is the per-part treatment (fitted or open, short, or a value) in the window. **Decided (advisor, 2026-11-20): the per-part list in the window (sidecar file) is the override; no footprint field now.** Reasons: the list is needed anyway (body treatment, fitted or open, lumped values); "may be open or closed" is a what-if per run, which is what the sidecar is for and becomes a sweep point under 11(g); the board API does not expose variants, which are KiCAD's proper home for it (read them when kipy does). If a footprint field (for example `EM` with `open`, `short`, `R=0`, `L=2.2n`) is ever added it is a read-only pre-fill for the row's default, shown as "from footprint field EM", with the same source order as the settings registry (user, field, parsed value, reference-prefix hint) and the source named per part in the run summary; deferred until a user asks for board-portable defaults.
    - **Ferrite bead.** Other tools use either a vendor S-parameter block placed at a port pair (HFSS-style co-simulation; Murata and others publish Touchstone files whose terms restrict use, so **we never ship them, the user supplies**) or a lumped equivalent circuit: a parallel R, L, C with a small series R, fitted from the datasheet impedance curve ([Altium](https://resources.altium.com/p/how-determine-ferrite-bead-circuit-model-parameters), [Cadence PSpice note](https://resources.pcb.cadence.com/application-notes/pspice-ferrite-bead-models-to-analyze-emi-suppression); a cascade of two parallel RLC sections is reported accurate from LF to UHF; the single-section model is trustworthy only near resonance; real beads lose inductance under DC bias, which is out of scope and stated). In FDTD, lumped elements on one Yee edge are naturally in parallel, so the bead's parallel RLC falls out of item 12(a) once the inductor state exists: three user-entered values, no new mechanism. The series resistance is a later case. S-parameter blocks stay the separate, already listed item that needs its own method review. Method: reference [7] (Piket-May, Taflove and Baron 1994, DOI 10.1109/22.297814) is the 3D lumped-element formulation; read its R, L, C sections before the inductor is written (not yet read for L and C).
    - **Order:** (1) export pads and attributes, with an exporter scenario on the fake board; (2) the run-summary warning that two-terminal parts are not modelled; (3) 0 ohm and R through the port-strip mechanism; (4) C; (5) L, then the bead as a parallel RLC (method: the auxiliary-differential-equation update of Demir [29], read; its inductor and parallel RLC cases checked numerically against 1/(1+jwR C)-style closed forms as for the capacitor; stability: no lumped-inductor criterion was read, so the scenario is energy non-increasing without a source at the smallest L the UI allows on the finest cell, and [31] is read before the inductor ships); (6) the net walk; (7) the per-part treatment list and the bodies in the preview.
13. **Mathematical operations: baseline, known-correct values, and where speed can come from (asked 2026-11-20, researched with the advisor).**
    - **What does not apply, so it does not come back.** Karatsuba, Toom-Cook and FFT multiplication are big-integer and polynomial methods: an IEEE float multiply is one hardware operation, and nothing here multiplies big integers. The Gauss three-multiplication complex product (the same idea as Karatsuba) saves one multiply but is stable only in norm, and the smaller component can have a large relative error or the wrong sign [32]; our hot path, the phasor accumulator, is a real value times a complex trig factor (two multiplies), and complex times complex appears only in reconstruction, S11 and the far-field sums, which are tiny. Not adopted. No FFT is needed today: S11 is a band-limited DFT at about 100 frequencies over about 20 000 samples (2 million multiply-adds in double); the far-field transform needs sums, not an FFT.
    - **Where the levers really are.** The solver is bandwidth-bound (CLAUDE.md, measured), so the levers are precision and memory traffic, not operation count: fp32 field storage against a double CPU reference (differential tests exist); compensated summation wherever a long fp32 sum lives (the accumulator has Kahan; the far-field sums get the same, or pairwise summation; the surface-current cut sums are short); host-side double trig tables for the accumulator phases (a deliberate design: no trig on the GPU); fewer bytes per step (the graded grid, tiling only if measured to help, half-precision storage only with a measured error budget). Goertzel is cheaper than an FFT for a few bins but less accurate [33]; the accumulator's direct DFT with Kahan is kept.
    - **Baseline.** Performance: `apps/bench/bench` (writes `out/bench.json`) under the GPU lock, warm, against the measured copy bandwidth; add the accumulator's share of the step time (not measured yet) and the reconstruction and render times already in the UI's frame timer. Correctness: the CPU double engine, a double-precision DFT reference, and the analytic scenarios already written (cavity modes, RC, RL, port reflection, the energy balance).
    - **Known-correct values.** The pattern in the literature is a higher-precision reference (MPFR) compared in ULP against a stated bound per function. Ours: an `mpmath` (BSD-3) generator script checked in next to golden JSON in `tests/data/`, regenerated on demand, never a runtime dependency; closed forms where they exist (the DFT of a Gaussian, 1 / (1 + j w R C), the RL divider). MPFR is LGPL: do not link it, even in tests. Boost.Multiprecision (Boost Software License 1.0) only if a C++ reference is ever needed. The fp32 sum tests state their bound in the THEN.
    - **Licensing, adopt nothing now (YAGNI); if an FFT is ever needed:** pocketfft (BSD-3 in the C++ header-only form), KISS FFT (BSD), FFTS (BSD) are compatible with MIT; VkFFT is reported MIT but its LICENSE file has not been read; FFTW is GPL-2 or a paid licence and is never used. Each would go in the Licensing table and CREDITS.md before use.
    - **The correctness gap to close first (advisor).** The GPU accumulator scenario (1e-4 of the peak, 60 samples) cannot tell Kahan from a naive fp32 sum. Add one: seed a node to 1.0, a phasor list at f = 0, one `accumulate` at weight 1e8, then 100 000 at weight 1, no stepping; the phasor must be 1.001e8 within 1e-6 (a naive fp32 sum stays at 1e8, whose ulp is 8), with the CPU bank as control. This also tests whether MoltenVK's shader compiler keeps the `precise` (no reassociation) promise; if it fails, check `MVK_CONFIG_FAST_MATH_ENABLED` and record the finding either way. Also print the device's float-control properties (denormal and NaN preservation, core Vulkan 1.2) in the run summary.
    - **Later, each with a scenario and a measured line:** pairwise or Kahan sums in the far-field transform; ULP table for the trig factors the host computes (double, so expected exact to rounding); a bench of the accumulator against the step kernel to decide whether a different sampling interval or channel layout is worth it; fp32 versus double CPU solver drift over 100 000 steps on the cavity (known resonances) as the precision budget for any lower-precision storage.
    - **Architecture for (a), decided to avoid rework:** one accumulator kernel over an **index list of sample points** with N frequencies and a per-list component mask. Display voxels, copper-face cells (b) and Huygens-box cells (f) are three lists feeding the same kernel. Poynting and surface current need H, so components are chosen per list: the dense display volume keeps one E component by default (three E plus three H over 128^3 at N = 15 would be about 1.5 GB). The sampling interval must satisfy the half-period rule at the top of the **excitation** band, not the reported band.
    - **Smith chart:** drawn with the existing plot helpers and given a spoken summary; implot is added only if those cannot carry it (the dependency checklist in Licensing applies).
    - **Scenario examples:** (a) the GPU accumulator and `viz::PhasorAccumulator` sample the same field every S steps and every phasor matches within 1e-4 of the peak magnitude; (b) surface current integrated across a strip matches the port current within a stated tolerance; (f) half-wave dipole peak gain within a tolerance of 2.15 dBi, set from a convergence test.
14. **GPU rendering follow-ups (2026-11-20; R1, R3 and R4 built, see "GPU rendering" and Measured so far).** In order: (a) **Move the build to the LunarG SDK** (`VULKAN_SDK`, its loader and `glslc`; the current SDK is installed system-wide in `/usr/local`, Homebrew's loader is still what CMake finds) and say in the README which driver is used; measure that nothing regresses on both drivers. (b) **Which driver is the default when `VK_ICD_FILENAMES` is unset** (not measured: with both ICDs installed the loader lists two devices; our device choice rule must pick one deliberately and the run summary must name the driver). (c) **Shader compile time and the first-frame stall** on both drivers (not measured). (d) **R2, no readback**, only if a 4K display or a bigger display volume makes the 3 to 5 ms per view matter; it needs the field images as GPU textures under the ImGui pass, which also removes the 8 MB upload per frame. (e) **Bundling for release:** which Vulkan driver the packaged app ships or requires (the SDK, KosmicKrisp from Mesa, or neither: ask the user), plus the licence text in `cmake/Package.cmake` and `CREDITS.md`; MoltenVK is Apache-2.0, KosmicKrisp is part of Mesa (check the file). (f) **Windows and Linux:** `imgui_impl_vulkan` and the swapchain overlay are portable by construction but untested there; `EMFDTD_VALIDATE=1` is the first tool to use. (g) **KosmicKrisp on larger runs** (one 160^3 benchmark and one 36 s patch run so far, equal to MoltenVK within noise). The Smith chart and plots are ImGui draw lists and so already GPU-drawn.
15. **Reference-guided antenna tuning. (a) and (b) partly BUILT 2026-11-21 as the window's "Antenna" tab (user decision: a separate section): `data/antenna_rules.json` (the plain W3008's keepout 4.00 x 4.25 mm and 80 x 37 mm test board from datasheet v2.7, plus a generic unverified entry), `board::checkAntenna` (`src/board/antenna.*`, scenarios in `tests/cpu/antenna_test.cpp`) and the tab. It reports, with no solve: copper of other parts or nets inside the keepout per layer (areas from a painted grid of 0.01 mm cells, so overlapping zones, pads and tracks are a union), vias inside it, the ground area, reach and width per layer against the vendor's test board, and the stackup under the part. The keepout is placed as datasheets draw it: centred on the part's pads, from the nearest board edge inward (an assumption stated on screen); size from the rule or the settings `antenna_keepout_along_mm` and `antenna_keepout_depth_mm`. **Configuration (built 2026-11-22):** the chosen part is saved in `em-fdtd/selection.json`; `em-fdtd/antenna_rules.json` adds a project's own rules (they win over the shipped ones); settings `antenna_keepout_placement` (rule, edge, corner), `antenna_testboard_length_mm` and `antenna_testboard_width_mm`; rules may carry `placement`. Documented in `docs/ux.md`, "Antenna tab". Not built yet: ground-net override, the feed line's Z0 for the stackup, the match network, nearby metal, via stitching along the keepout edge, the two-run overlay (c), the match suggestions (d). Checked on a synthetic board (17.00 mm2 in a 17 mm2 keepout, 3600 mm2 of a 60 x 60 mm ground), not yet on the user's real board through the plugin. Original proposal: (proposed 2026-11-21, see "Reference-guided antenna tuning"; not started, needs the user's answers there).** Order: (a) `data/antenna_rules.json` with the read and cited values for the part variants in use; (b) the Tier 1 keepout, ground and feed-Z0 checker with its fixtures (scenarios first), including via layer spans; (c) the S11 and surface-current overlay of two runs (shares item 11 and the sweep overlay); (d) the match-network suggestion list (after item 11 (e), the Smith chart); (e) Tier 3 only after a method review. Before (a)-(e) are relied on, the chip-geometry test under "Is the W3008's radiator just its visible Ag metallization?" (one run at one permittivity first; about 35 to 60 minutes of the shared GPU each) decides whether the chip can be a body-plus-caps model. The example board's own findings go to `out/` and are never committed.
16. **Reload a saved run without solving again: BUILT 2026-11-21 (details below are the design; this paragraph is the status).** Each run now writes `run.json` (manifest) and `fields/` (`slice_e`, `volume_e`, `current_h`, `power_eh`, `ntff` as `.f16` phasor files, and `farfield.json`) unless the setting `save_fields` is off; `results/index.json` is a cache rebuilt by `app::scanRuns`. `emfdtd BOARD.json --open-run RUNDIR [--ui | --ui-screenshot ...]` rebuilds the model from the run's own `board.json` and `settings.json` (no solve, no GPU lock; use `--cpu` for screenshots), loads the fields and the S11 and shows the Done screen; `--prune-runs N` deletes the field data of all but the newest N runs and keeps their S11. In the window, "Open run..." (Preview and results screens; user decision 2026-11-21) lists the runs (time, board, best match, fields kept or not, size) and has a button to drop the fields of older runs (keep the newest 3). Checked on the patch sample at 1000x700: the S11, slice and far-field screens of a reload look the same as the live run (12 MB of fields per run, reload 0.5 s against 25 s); the 3D volume screen was not compared. **Limits:** the model is rebuilt from the board and the settings, so a run made with a model selection (picked nets, `--include`, `--refine`) that is not restored is refused with a message when the saved field sizes do not fit; the selection is not yet stored in the run folder. Probes and sweeps are not reloaded (probes.csv and sweep.json stay as files). Original design notes follow. **Reload a saved run without solving again (asked for by the user 2026-11-21: every UI check and every re-look currently repeats a solve, which wastes GPU or CPU time).** Today a results folder holds only `board.json`, `s11.s1p`, `settings.json` and `summary.txt`; the viewer's data (the multi-frequency phasors of E, and H for surface current, the far-field figures, the grid and the copper masks the overlays need) is not written, so nothing can be reopened. Needed: (a) write the phasors at display resolution to the run folder as binary (fp16 after the run, as the Animation section proposes) with a small manifest (grid, frequencies, components, solver version); (b) `emfdtd --open-run DIR` that fills the same viewer state from the folder with no solve and no GPU lock (a window and screenshot path, testable by scenario: a saved run reloads to the same picture as the live one); (c) only after the user decides the UX, a window action to open a saved run (proposal: an "Open a saved run..." button on the Preview screen and a list of recent runs for the board). **Decided by the user 2026-11-21:** fields are kept by default (a setting turns it off; a prune command removes the field data of old runs and keeps their S11), and the list of past runs lives in each project's own folder. Layout (next to the board, never inside the KiCAD files, in the existing `em-fdtd/` folder): `em-fdtd/results/run-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS/` holds `run.json` (the manifest: schema version, solver version, board name, a hash of the snapshot, the settings that differed from the profile, band, grid, step count, wall time, S11 summary, whether fields are saved), `s11.s1p`, `summary.txt`, `board.json`, `settings.json` and `fields/` (`manifest.json` plus one binary per quantity, fp16, little-endian: `e_phasors.f16`, `h_phasors.f16`, the far-field tables, the copper masks the overlays need); sweeps stay in `em-fdtd/results/sweep-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS/` with one run folder per point; `em-fdtd/results/index.json` lists the runs (id, time, status, S11 summary, size, has-fields) and is a cache rebuilt by scanning the run folders, so deleting or moving a folder never corrupts it. The existing `results/` name is kept (renaming would orphan every earlier run). Until then, UI checks should solve once on the GPU under the lock and take every screenshot they need from that one run.
17. **Plugin lessons from the antenna study (2026-11-21, section "Plugin lessons from the antenna study")**: items 1, 2, 3 and 6 built (net-touch check, port-path check, thin and touching body notes, model labels); item 4 partly built ("Excluding breakaway tabs": slot-end bridging is in the exporter, `outline.board_proper`, seeded at the median footprint position, with a note and no switch; next perforation rows, then the opening fallback, then the Preview line and the switch once the user decides); item 5 partly built as the Antenna tab (Next steps 15 (a) and (b)); the remaining checks of item 5 are listed there.

(b) The user's real surroundings are a small aluminium USB adapter with the antenna end sticking out about 3 to 4 mm (see "Alternative chip antennas", Enclosure): model the shell and a host ground as metal bodies once the protrusion length and the shell-to-ground contact are known. (c) 3D models and CAD links for the alternative antennas could not be checked on DigiKey, Mouser or Octopart (automated fetches blocked; the browser extension was not connected): check with the user's accounts, then read the STEP files with the `antenna-part-sources` skill. (d) Not built: feed Z0 for the stackup, match network and suggestions, nearby metal, the two-run overlay, perforation-row and opening-by-width tab exclusion, the run's model selection stored in its folder, `--open-run` keeping `--set` values, a comparison of a reloaded 3D volume screen with a live run, the GPU 3D renderer's colour-map animation (tested on the CPU renderer only). (e) A generic NanoVNA checklist (`docs/vna-measurement.md`) exists locally and is deliberately not committed yet; board-specific measurement drawings stay private in `out/`. (f) CI on GitHub was last checked green at `c91367f`; later pushes were checked locally with `scripts/check.sh` only.

## nRF52840 + W3008 reference candidate (built 2026-11-22; private, unmeasured)
**Why.** A known-working nRF52840 design to see how the plugin behaves. `../bomu-hardware` has no such design (its board is the 2 to 3 ft one; the rest are vendor part files and antenna-only boards), so Nordic's reference layout was used. Nordic publishes it for QIAA and CKAA as Altium files only; the download page is behind a bot check, so the user fetched the QIAA 1.3 zip (the PDF's CKAA circuits have schematics but no layout). No licence file is in the zip, so everything derived stays git-ignored (`boards/nrf52840-w3008/`, `out/nrf52840-ref/`).
**What.** KiCad's own Altium importers (PCB through `pcbnew` Python, schematic through the GUI; no headless schematic route was found) give Nordic's config 6 as a 22.55 x 19.55 mm four-layer board with the stack kept (1.6 mm; 0.07, 1.3, 0.07 mm dielectrics). The generator in `boards/nrf52840-w3008/` extends it to the W3008 test-board outline (80 x 37 mm), adds the W3008 at its datasheet position with its all-layer clearance, a pi placeholder (0R series, shunts not fitted) and an unfitted U.FL tap, and links footprints and net names to the schematic. The antenna is the W3008 because it is the only antenna in `../bomu-hardware/3rd-party`; the footprint is this repository's own datasheet-derived one.
**What it can and cannot test.** The W3008 chip stays not modellable here (see "Reference-guided antenna tuning"), so this board tests the rule checks (keepout copper per layer, ground size, via spans, feed impedance, net separation) and a feed-and-ground solve, not antenna S11. Nordic's RF line is a 0.762 mm top-layer coplanar line with the In1 and In2 copper voided under it, so the reference plane is B.Cu 1.6 mm below; that is the closed-form cross-check for the feed, to run with `boards/w3008-testboard/cpwg.py` on the imported stack.
**Findings.** (1) Nordic's schematic leaves crystal X1 pins 2 and 4 unconnected where the layout grounds them. (2) The Altium rules are finer than the W3008 board's fab limits (0.145 mm track/space, 0.35/0.15 mm vias). (3) KiCad's Python: `ZONE.SetOutline` takes ownership and double-frees (edit `Outline()` in place), and `kicad-cli` cannot import Altium schematics. (4) A first version had the top and In1 pours silently empty from exactly that; the DRC's 21 unconnected items caught it, so check fills after any generator change.
**Open.** Plugin export and `fdtd-control-check` before any S11; CKAA reference not obtained; nothing built or measured.

**Trace-antenna variant (built 2026-11-22; private, unmeasured).** The user asked for a modelable antenna, so `boards/nrf52840-ifa/` swaps the W3008 for TI's SWRU120D inverted-F (plain copper, about 25.6 x 6.9 mm, published return loss and gain to compare with). Same Nordic block and checks; the IFA geometry is Table 1-1 plus leg positions read off Figure 1-1 (consistent with the table to about 0.05 mm). The feed and the grounded legs are one piece of copper, so the footprint uses net-tie pads. TI tuned it on 1 mm FR-4 and we use 1.6 mm 4-layer with an all-layer keepout under it, so a shifted match is expected. TI's terms allow use only for developing an application with TI products: keep git-ignored. DRC on a copy: 0 unconnected, no clearance or short errors. Next: plugin export, `fdtd-control-check`, then one solve against TI's plot; whether the model reproduces TI's band at a 1 mm stack is the real test of the plugin on a modelable antenna.

**IFA board, plugin export and controls (2026-11-22).** Export through the plugin worked on the open board (403 copper polygons, 179 vias). The run notes were right: they flagged that the feed's two terminals are joined by copper (the antenna's grounded legs, expected for a shunt-fed IFA) and that `/ANT_FEED` and `GND` touch on F.Cu at the legs only (`net_separation.py`: x 184.1 to 187.4 mm, y 107.5 to 107.8 mm, nowhere near the feed gap). Net names carry a leading slash (`/ANT_FEED`, `/RF`); `--part R2=open` is needed or the net walk crosses the 0R and pulls in the whole Nordic block. **Known-load control FAILED:** port FEED at (186.8 to 188.04, 105.4) mm on F.Cu, antenna copper removed (open) and a GND tie across the feed 2.3 mm from the port (short), coarse mesh, decay 0.01, about 140 s each (`out/nrf52840-ifa/em-fdtd/results/run-20261122-191215` open, `run-20261122-191436` short). Both read as a short: phase at f=0 of 180.0 and 180.1 deg, |S11| 0.99, slope -2.9 and -4.5 deg/GHz (an open must read near 0). Ruled out so far: touching nets near the port (none), port terminals on connected copper (yes), In1 and In2 voided under the port (checked in the snapshot). Not yet found: the likely suspects are the port sitting in a 0.762 mm line with a 0.14 mm pour clearance and the 0.07 mm F.Cu to In1 dielectric (In1 is voided there, but the voxel model has not been looked at), or an effective short a little over 1 mm from the port. No antenna S11 from this board is to be trusted until an open control reads open. Next: look at the model view of the control, then move the port to a straight section away from the corner and retry.

**IFA control failure: cause found (2026-11-22).** Three separate defects, in the order found. (1) **Unresolved coplanar gap (solver blind spot, fixed).** The 0.15 to 0.2 mm gap between the 0.762 mm feed line and the F.Cu ground pour is under one cell in the coarse, medium and fine presets (the smallest cell, 0.0232 mm, exists only at the port), so the line's edges and the pour's edges meet at a grid node. That is a connection, yet the touch note stayed silent because it only fired when two nets claimed the same planar edge. The port-path note did say "joined by copper" on the vertical-port runs; the in-plane-port runs said nothing and read as a short (phase 180 deg, |S11| 0.99), the vertical-port runs read 160 deg. Found by bisecting the snapshot with dry runs (remove vias, the F.Cu pour, each ANT_FEED polygon): the joined note vanished without vias or without the pour, and with `--refine 186.4 104.4 189.2 108.2 0.06` (3 cells across the gap); the 0.05 mm raster of `net_separation.py` could not see it either. **Fix:** `voxelize` now claims the end nodes of every planar copper edge as well as the edges, so nets meeting at a node are reported; scenario "a feed track and a ground pour with a gap of 0.3 mm, under the 1 mm cell" (red first, then green; the whole CPU suite passes, 108 cases). Rule: on a coplanar feed, put a `--refine` box with cells of at most a third of the gap around it, and treat a "joined by copper" note on an open control as this defect. (2) **Control design.** A port in the middle of a line sees the long open line on its other side: the first refined short read 56 deg with the same -120 deg/GHz slope as the open. Controls must end the line at the port (cut the copper beyond it). (3) **The short tie missed the pour.** My tie sat below the pour's edge (the strip keepout and the pad clearance), so it touched only the feed line: the first three "short" runs were never shorts. Check that the short control's dry run says "joined by copper". Refined open control with the line cut: see below.

**Antenna skills and helper scripts (2026-11-22).** Skills in `.claude/skills/`: `antenna-matching`, `antenna-placement`, `antenna-debug` (new), beside `antenna-reference-compare`, `antenna-part-sources`, `fdtd-control-check` (extended with how to build readable controls) and `vendor-reference-board`. They are generic: no repository or board is named. Their helpers are in `scripts/antenna/` (standard library only, tracked, self-test `python3 scripts/antenna/selftest.py`, run by `scripts/check.sh`): `match.py` (S11 summary, L-match values, network evaluation on a Touchstone load, E12 pi and T search), `lines.py` (microstrip and grounded-coplanar Z0, width solve, wavelengths), `touchstone.py`. Limits: ideal lumped parts, quasi-static zero-thickness lines (the coplanar formula is the same Simons chapter 2 closed form as the private `cpwg.py`, not re-read); sources, what was checked and what was read (2026-11-22). **DOIs checked on Crossref (title, authors, year, pages):** Hammerstad and Jensen 1980 (10.1109/MWSYM.1980.1124303), Simons 2001 chapter 3 "Conductor-Backed Coplanar Waveguide" (10.1002/0471224758.ch3; the formula is in chapter 3, not 2 as first written), Fano 1950 part I (10.1016/0016-0032(50)90006-8; part II's guessed DOI 404s and is not cited). **Not read, because they are paywalled:** all three, and Pozar's textbook (no DOI found). The equations were instead checked against open secondary sources that were read: the Qucs Technical Documentation pages "Single microstrip line" (Hammerstad and Jensen equations) and "Coplanar waveguides (CPW)" (conductor-backed CPW: q, k1, k3, epsilon_re and Z, identical to `lines.py cpwg` once its exact K/K' ratio replaces their approximation), and F4INX's "Microstrip formulas comparison" (2024; same Hammerstad and Jensen set; it prints the last term of a(u) without the cube, the Qucs page with it, so the two secondary sources disagree by at most 0.3 percent of epsilon_eff, and the code uses the cube). **Found by reading:** the first version of `lines.py` mixed Hammerstad and Jensen's 1980 impedance with the older Hammerstad 1975 effective permittivity; fixed (the 50 ohm width on 1.6 mm FR-4 at er 4.4 is still 3.06 mm). Schnieder, Tischler and Heinrich, IEEE T-MTT 51(1), 2003, was read in full (open author copy at fbh-berlin.de): it is about dispersion and radiation of conductor-backed CPW with finite ground width and gives no quasi-static formulas (it points to Heinrich 1993 and Ghione and Naldi 1987, both unread). Fano's open MIT report (RLE Technical Report 41, 1948, hdl.handle.net/1721.1/5007, no DOI) was downloaded by the user and read in full (all 48 pages; the figure pages do not extract as text): it supports the matching skill's claims (a bandwidth limit for a given load and tolerance; Bode's parallel RC integral, eqs. 3 and 4; a deep match at one frequency narrows the band; the lossless-network assumption; the resonant-antenna example in appendix II). The 1950 journal version (the Crossref-checked DOI) remains unread. To close the gap the user can place the three papers or the book chapter in `out/antennas/` (they are licensed to the reader, not to us).

**IFA known-load controls PASS (2026-11-22, after the three fixes above).** Snapshot `out/nrf52840-ifa/w_open.json` and `w_short.json`: ANT_FEED copper cut so the line ends at the port (vertical port, F.Cu to B.Cu at (188.04, 105.0) mm, 0.3 mm wide), antenna removed, `--nets /ANT_FEED --ground-nets GND --part R2=open --set mesh_preset=coarse --set decay_threshold=0.01 --refine 186.4 104.2 189.2 108.2 0.06`; the short is a GND tie 0.3 mm tall from the pour (x 187.0 mm) across the feed at y 106.7 mm, 1.7 mm from the port. Open (`run-20261122-202835`): phase at f=0 325 deg, no "joined" note; short (`run-20261122-203810`): 183 deg, "joined by copper" note present; each about 575 s at 437 Mcells/s with the GPU to itself (a contended run took 1696 s). Both are within the skill's 45 degree tolerance. **Not yet done:** the antenna itself (full model with the IFA, port at the 0R pad or the feed line), a halved-cell check, any comparison with TI's published return loss. The earlier runs on this board (`run-20261122-191215` to `run-20261122-195928`) are invalid for the reasons above and are kept only as the record.

**Closed-form line impedance checked against the solver (2026-11-22; `scripts/antenna/validate_lines.py`, `out/lines-check/`).** Why: the Hammerstad and Jensen paper and the Simons chapter are paywalled and unread, so `lines.py` is checked against this project's own FDTD instead. Method: a 30 mm line on a 1.6 mm, er 4.4, lossless board, lumped vertical port (a third of the strip width) at one end, open far end; Z0 and k fitted to the phase of S11 (Z_in = j w Lp - j Z0 cot(k f); the port's series inductance came out 0.03 to 1 nH). **Results with the default mesh:** 50 ohm microstrip 44.4 ohm (-11 percent against the formula's 50.0), 1 mm microstrip 67.4 against 87.5 (-23), coplanar with ground (w 0.8, gap 0.3 mm) 21 against 64.7 (-67). **Convergence on the 50 ohm microstrip (formula 50.0 ohm, eff. permittivity 3.33):** lateral cell 0.25 to 0.125 mm: 44.4 to 43.6 ohm; 8 to 16 cells through the board: 44.3; **a fine air cell next to the board (`air_near_cell_mm` 0.2, `air_near_height_mm` 3): 50.9 ohm**; 0.1 mm cell over 4 mm: 51.2; fine air plus 0.125 mm lateral: 50.1 ohm (+0.1 percent). So the default air meshing next to the board under-resolves the field above a planar line and reads its impedance about 11 percent low; the formula was right. With the fine air layer: 1 mm microstrip 87.0 against 87.5 (-0.5 percent); coplanar gap 0.3 mm (3 cells across the gap) 55.0 against 64.7 (-14.9), gap 0.5 mm (5 cells) 67.7 against 73.3 (-7.7): the error falls as the gap gets more cells, so it is the solver's gap resolution (extrapolating, about 10 cells across the gap would close it; not run). **What is established:** `lines.py` microstrip Z0 agrees with the solver to about 2 percent once the air is resolved (50 ohm, 87 ohm cases); the coplanar formula is consistent with the solver's convergence but not confirmed to better than the 8 percent seen at 5 cells. **Not established:** effective permittivity (fit 4.45 default, 3.86 at the finest, formula 3.33 and falling; the fit assumes a 0.4 h open-end extension and a port with no stub, either may hide a few percent); the narrow-line and coplanar cases at more cells; conductor thickness (the formulas and the solver both use zero thickness). **Consequences:** (1) a feed's impedance in a run depends on the air cell above it: for a planar feed or antenna set `air_near_cell_mm` and `air_near_height_mm`, as the control-check skill now says; (2) the real boards' coplanar gaps (Nordic's 0.15 mm) are only 2 to 3 cells wide at a 0.06 mm refinement, so the simulated feed impedance there is not trustworthy to better than tens of percent; (3) **open decision for the user:** whether the default mesh should put a fine air layer next to the board whenever the board has copper on the top layer (it costs 1.5 to 2 times the cells in these runs).

**Air meshing next to the board: recommendation (2026-11-22; advisor and research; awaiting the user's decision, nothing changed in the solver).** *Diagnosis.* The default mesh breaks the smooth-mesh rule at the board to air interface: in the microstrip runs the coarse cell is 2.38 mm (boundary 69 mm, 30 cells per side) and the dielectric is cut into 0.2 mm cells, so the first air cell above the top copper is 12 times the cell below it, at the plane where a microstrip's fringing field lives. The openEMS FDTD mesh page (wiki.openems.de/index.php/FDTD_Mesh.html, CC BY-SA, read; a GPL code, documentation only, nothing copied) states the rule: neighbouring cells should not differ by more than about a factor 2, the largest cell should be below lambda_min/15, and strips need metal mesh lines (its "thirds rule" puts a line 1/3 inside and 2/3 outside a metal edge to improve line impedance). *Measured (same 50 ohm microstrip, formula 50.0 ohm):* default (no growth, ratio 12 at the interface) 44.4 ohm; air cell 0.2 mm over 3 mm then growth 1.3: 50.9 ohm, 3.06 M cells; over 1 mm: 50.9 ohm, 2.53 M cells; **over 0.2 mm, i.e. one fine cell then growth 1.3: 50.8 ohm (+1.6 percent), 2.29 M cells against 1.94 M default (+18 percent cells, 78 against 66 z cells, no change of the smallest cell or time step)**; a 0.1 mm air cell made it no better (51.2 ohm) and cost a smaller time step. So the cure is grading the air from the dielectric's cell size outward at a bounded ratio, not a fine layer of a given thickness. *Recommendation (option B):* build the air above and below the board with cells growing by a ratio (default 1.3, the growth `airOffsets` already uses after a near layer) from the outermost dielectric cell up to the coarse cell, as a rule of the mesher and not an `air_near_*` setting; expose the ratio as a registry setting (`mesh_growth_ratio`) and keep `air_near_cell_mm` for thin component bodies. Scenario first: a grid plan with dielectric cell d and coarse air cell larger than 2 d yields no adjacent z cells whose ratio exceeds the setting; then the change; then `validate_lines.py --case microstrip` at the new default must give 50 ohm within 3 percent. Option A (fine layer whenever the top has copper) costs about twice as much in these runs and is not general. Option C (leave the default and document the setting) leaves every user's feed impedance 11 percent low. *Not part of this decision:* the coplanar gap error (3 cells: -15 percent, 5 cells: -8 percent) is lateral resolution and wants its own rule (a refine box with cells of at most a tenth of the gap around coplanar feeds); the thirds rule is a later accuracy item (the lateral-refinement test showed it is not the dominant error here); the effective-permittivity gap (fit 3.86 against 3.33 at the finest) is unresolved, partly real dispersion (the fit spans 0.5 to 3 GHz and the effective permittivity of a microstrip rises with frequency, per the FDTD microstrip literature found in the search; those papers were not read). Peer-reviewed grading studies the search found (nonuniform-grid microstrip FDTD, IEEE and JPIER papers) were not read.

**Air meshing next to the board: DONE (2026-11-22, the user approved option B).** `GridPlan::airGrowth` (setting `mesh_growth_ratio`, advanced, Mesh group, default 1.3, range 1 to 2) grades the air above and below the board from the adjacent board cell's size up to the coarse cell (`airOffsets` in `src/board/voxelize.cpp`); a fine layer (`air_near_*`) still starts at its own cell and always grew by 1.3. Scenario first ("The air next to the board grows from the board's own cell, by no more than the growth ratio": red at a step of 2.5, then green); `kPlan` in `tests/cpu/board_test.cpp` keeps `airGrowth = 1` because other scenarios count z nodes in an even air layout. The whole CPU suite passes (109 cases). **Measured at the new default:** the 50 ohm microstrip of `validate_lines.py` fits 50.8 ohm (+1.6 percent against 50.0; requirement was within 3 percent), 2.29 M cells against 1.94 M (+18 percent), smallest cell and time step unchanged. Not rerun at the new default: the narrow microstrip and the coplanar cases, the 3D and slice views, any earlier recorded S11 of a planar feed or antenna (they will differ). Still open: the coplanar gap rule (a refine box with cells of at most a tenth of the gap), the thirds rule, the effective-permittivity gap.

**Plugin updated with the findings (2026-11-22).** (1) `mesh_growth_ratio` (Mesh group, Advanced) is in the settings registry, so the solver window lists it, searches it ("growth"), saves it in the board's `em-fdtd/settings.json` and profiles, and gives it an accessibility node ("Mesh growth next to the board", checked in `--ui-show-settings --ui-query growth` screenshot and a11y dump); the KiCAD plugin launches that window and reads the same settings file, so no plugin Python changed. (2) New run note, scenario first (red, then green, CPU suite 110 cases): a gap of at most 4 cells between two nets within 4 mm of a port is reported with its width in cells and mm and the advice to refine (`voxelize`, after the net-touch note). On the IFA board's feed it reports the 0.3 mm feed-to-pour gap as 1 cell wide without a refine box and 4 cells with the 0.06 mm box. (3) The net-touch check also claims grid nodes. Not done: a window control that adds refine boxes from the note (the `--refine` flag and Fine cell near ports exist; a per-gap box list is item 4 of the refinement design in `docs/ux.md` and needs a UX decision), the feed-impedance row in the Antenna tab (the closed forms in `scripts/antenna/lines.py` are validated to about 2 percent for microstrip, coplanar not confirmed; a C++ port needs the same scenarios and the user's go-ahead), a note for the effective-permittivity gap.

**Feed impedance row and refinement suggestions: BUILT (2026-11-22, on the user's request to implement the "Not done" items).** *Feed row* (Antenna tab, `board::checkFeed` in `src/board/antenna.cpp`, closed forms in `src/board/line_impedance.*`): from the part's first non-ground pad it flood-fills the connected copper of that net on the part's layer (0.02 mm raster, 16 mm window), measures the width (shorter run, median of samples 1 to 6 mm from the pad) and the gap to other-net copper across the line (coplanar when found at both sides for at least half the samples), finds the reference plane (nearest copper layer whose ground copper lies under at least 80 percent of the samples; the dielectric between is summed and its permittivity is the series-capacitance equivalent), and computes Z0 by the Hammerstad and Jensen microstrip form or the grounded-coplanar conformal-mapping form (exact elliptic integrals). Scenarios first: 3.06 mm over 1.6 mm gives 50 ohm within 5 percent, a 0.8 mm line with 0.3 mm gaps gives about 65 ohm, missing feed copper and missing ground plane are reported. The same C++ and `scripts/antenna/lines.py` agree (54.6 ohm for 0.762 mm, 0.154 mm gap, 1.44 mm, er 4.5). On the IFA board's feed it reads 0.76 mm wide, 0.20 mm gap (the pour clearance), 1.44 mm of er 4.5, grounded coplanar, 58 ohm, +17 percent against the 50 ohm port. *Refinement* (`src/board/refine_suggest.*`, Preview tab, sidecar `refinement`): see `docs/ux.md` item 4; on the IFA control board it suggests a 0.033 mm box (0.2 mm gap, 6 cells) that takes the grid from 0.9 M to 5.0 M cells and the estimate from about 190 s to about 1430 s, and makes the run notes' \"gap is N cells wide\" and \"joined by copper\" lines disappear; nothing is applied unless switched on. Shared `board::Raster` (`src/board/raster.hpp`) moved out of `antenna.cpp`. **GPU:** neither piece uses it: the closed forms are a few microseconds and the gap scan is a 0.01 mm raster of a 8 mm window per layer per port (milliseconds on the CPU); the GPU's value here is the solve the refinement feeds, which is why the estimate is shown before the run. A 2D field solve of the feed's cross-section (Laplace on a grid, a compute shader) would be the GPU-shaped way to get coplanar impedance without the closed form's limits; it is not built (it needs its own CPU reference and scenarios, and the closed forms are within their stated accuracy for the common lines). Not done: a per-gap interactive box editor beyond the cell field; the feed row for bends, stubs and vias; the effective-permittivity gap.

**2D cross-section solve: CPU reference and scenarios BUILT (2026-11-22; the GPU kernel is not built).** *What:* `src/fdtd/line2d.{hpp,cpp}` (`fdtd::line2d`, pure logic): the quasi-static impedance and effective permittivity of any planar line cross-section, without the closed forms' limits (infinitely thin copper, symmetric side grounds, infinite substrate). Laplace's equation div(eps grad phi) = 0 on square cells, phi on the nodes, eps on the cells, a link carrying the mean permittivity of the cells it borders; conductor rectangles (sheets or real thickness) held at fixed potentials, walls grounded or mirrored; capacitance per metre from the stored energy; solved with the dielectrics and in air, eps_eff = C / C0 and Z0 = 1 / (c sqrt(C C0)). The iteration is the one a GPU would run: Jacobi (two buffers) or red-black over-relaxation (two fully parallel half-sweeps), selectable, so a compute shader can be differenced against this file. `lineImpedanceExtrapolated` solves at h and h/2 and extrapolates each capacitance as 2 C(h/2) - C(h). *Why extrapolate (measured):* a thin conductor's edge makes the field singular, so the plain solve converges only linearly with the cell: an air coplanar line (2 mm, 0.4 mm gaps, ground 4 mm below, closed form 91.27 ohm) read 80.5, 86.0, 88.6 and 89.9 ohm at cells of 0.2, 0.1, 0.05 and 0.025 mm (errors 10.8, 5.3, 2.7, 1.4: halving), and 91.36 extrapolated from 0.1. The FDTD validation's coplanar errors (15 percent low at 3 cells across the gap, 7.7 percent at 5) are this same first-order error. *Scenarios* (`tests/cpu/line2d_test.cpp`, written red first): parallel plates exact to 1e-6 (mirror walls, no fringing); Jacobi and red-black agree to 1e-5 and over-relaxation needs 5 times fewer sweeps; the plain error halves with the cell and the extrapolation is within 3 percent of the closed form; microstrip 3.06 mm within 2 percent (50.16 against 50.02 ohm, eps_eff 3.285 against 3.331) and 1.0 mm within 2 percent (87.0 against 87.5, walls eight line-widths away: with walls at four widths it read 85.9, so grounded walls bias the impedance low by about 1.5 percent); coplanar on FR-4 within 2 percent (64.66 against 64.69 ohm for 0.8 mm and 0.3 mm gaps, 73.22 against 73.3 for 0.5 mm); one-sided ground raises Z0 by 17 percent (76.2 against 65.0), which no closed form here can say; 0.05 mm of copper lowers it by 7 percent; bad sections are refused. The heavy scenarios are `[slow]` (the sanitizer build takes minutes; release `cpu_slow` runs all 17 slow cases in 2.7 minutes); `tests_cpu "[.report]"` prints the numbers below. *Findings:* (1) **a vendor RF line**: 0.762 mm wide, 0.154 mm gaps, 1.44 mm of er 4.5 (the Nordic reference's coplanar line) is 56.8 ohm as a copper sheet (closed form 54.6) and **50.1 ohm with 38.5 um of copper**, so its designer's 50 ohm holds once the copper has thickness; the zero-thickness closed form, and with it the feed row's flag (+17 percent on the IFA board's 0.20 mm gap, 58 ohm), overstates a coplanar line's impedance by 6 to 9 percent (the feed row's text now says so; the snapshot carries no copper thickness, so the row cannot correct for it yet). (2) The FDTD lines of `validate_lines.py`, solved quasi-statically: 3.06 mm microstrip 49.5 ohm (FDTD with graded air 50.1 to 50.9), 1.0 mm 87.0 (FDTD 87.0), coplanar 0.3 mm gap 64.7 (FDTD 55.0 at 3 cells across the gap), 0.5 mm gap 73.2 (FDTD 67.7 at 5 cells): the microstrip FDTD agrees within 2 percent, and the coplanar shortfall is the gap resolution, as concluded. *Method sources:* finite-difference electrostatics, the energy method for capacitance, successive over-relaxation with the model-problem optimum omega = 2 / (1 + sin(pi / n)) and Richardson extrapolation are textbook numerical analysis; no paper was consulted and none is cited. *Not built:* the GPU kernel (a Jacobi or red-black compute shader over the same arrays, compared against this reference), a graded grid (would cut the cost of the extrapolation: the CPU reference needs about 2 to 40 seconds a line in release), copper thickness in the snapshot and the exporter, and wiring the solve into the Antenna tab's feed row as an "accurate" estimate (it needs the thickness and a cross-section built from the measured width, gap, plane and stackup; `groundedCoplanarSection` already takes them).

**Plan for the four "Not built" 2D-solve items (2026-11-23; each is marked BUILT below when it lands).** Order: 3, 1, 4, 2 (the feed row needs the thickness and a fast solve; the graded grid is an optimisation of the CPU fallback and the riskiest numerically, so it goes last). *Item 3, copper thickness:* optional `thickness` (m, 0 or absent = unknown) on copper stackup entries of the snapshot, read from the KiCAD stackup by the exporter (copper's thickness sits on the stackup layer itself, as for dielectrics), the fab profile filling a missing value and saying so; the settings `copper_outer_um` / `copper_inner_um` keep their meaning (a value the user typed wins, then the snapshot's, then the profile's). The model keeps copper as sheets; the feed row's 2D solve uses the thickness. *Item 1, GPU kernel:* red-black over-relaxation in float32 (Metal has no fp64), the kernel reading link conductances and held-node flags, not permittivities, so the graded grid (item 2) changes only the rasteriser and never the shader; batches of sweeps, residual and capacitance computed on the CPU in double; scenario `[gpu]`: capacitance within 0.5 percent of the double CPU reference. `Raster` and `rasterise` move to the header so both backends share them. *Item 4, Antenna tab:* build the section from the measured width, gap, height, permittivity and thickness (symmetric side grounds, said in the row), solve off the draw thread (GPU when the window has a device, else CPU), cached on its inputs, showing "computing" until done; the headless screenshot path waits. *Item 2, graded grid:* tensor-product spacings, conductance = eps times the perpendicular spacing over the along spacing; scenarios: uniform spacing reproduces the uniform solver exactly, a graded coplanar case lands within 0.5 percent of the uniform extrapolated value on a fraction of the nodes. No online research is needed: all four are numerical engineering on the method already recorded above.

**3D view check on the full IFA snapshot (2026-11-23, prompted by the user).** The field of `out/nrf52840-ifa/final/em-fdtd/snapshot.json` (run `--profile bluetooth --set mesh_preset=coarse`, volume at 2.448 GHz) is a broad cloud around the whole board, and the user's reading is right: it emanates from where the port is, not from the antenna. Cause, from the snapshot and the run notes: the only port in that snapshot (P1, 0.5 mm, 50 ohm) sits at (152.05, 103.9) mm on the chip side, 36 mm from the IFA (ANT1 at 188.09, 107.57); the model keeps every net, and its notes list GND vias landing on signal copper (XC2, DEC4_6, the NFC pads, P0.00/XL1 and more: "short-circuits them") and 74 more touching net pairs, so the excitation spreads into a shorted tangle of copper and the board radiates as a whole. The best S11 of that run (-4.3 dB at 2.856 GHz; -2.2 dB at 2.611 GHz coarse) is therefore not an antenna result, and nothing in this plan treats it as one. The valid test is the one the controls use: select the nets (`--nets /ANT_FEED --ground-nets GND`), the port at the feed line (`c_full.json`: FEED across the 1.2 mm gap at y 105.4 mm), the 0R open or fitted, graded air and a fine box at the feed. Open items from this: (1) the generator puts GND vias onto other nets' copper on the full board (a layout defect in `boards/nrf52840-ifa`, to fix before any full-board run); (2) a view that says where the excitation is (mark the port in the 3D and slice views; it is drawn as a red dot in the 3D view, but a slice with the field maximum marked would answer "where does it come from" directly).

**Graded grid moves up, and why (2026-11-23).** Building the feed row (item 4) exposed an error larger than the thing it was meant to show: `rasterise` snaps conductor edges to the nearest node, so a 0.154 mm gap is 2, 4 or 8 cells wide depending on the cell, and the extrapolated impedance of the Nordic line swings with it (sheet 50.1, 57.7 and 54.4 ohm at cells of 0.07, 0.035 and 0.019 mm; with 38.5 um of copper 43.5, 53.6 and 49.8). The 56.75 / 50.08 pair recorded above came from a cell that happened to divide the gap. The fix is the graded grid of item 2, made to carry features exactly: grid lines at every conductor edge and layer interface (so widths, gaps, thickness and heights are exact at any cell), the cell filled in between, optionally growing away from the features (geometric growth, capped), and link conductances eps times perpendicular size over length. Scenarios first: the Nordic geometry agrees within 3 percent across cells of 0.07, 0.035 and 0.019 mm; a graded solve lands within 0.5 percent of the uniform solve at the same finest cell on under a third of the nodes; uniform spacing on a whole-cell geometry keeps every existing scenario.

**Not-built items, status (2026-11-23).** (3) *Copper thickness: BUILT.* Optional `thickness` on snapshot copper entries (`docs/snapshot.md`), read by the exporter from the KiCAD stackup layer (pytest scenarios), user value over board value over profile value (`applyCopperThickness`, C++ scenarios), carried into `FeedReport::copperMm`. (2) *Graded grid: BUILT, and it was the key to the feed row's accuracy.* Grid lines on every conductor edge and interface, geometric growth capped at 8 cells (`GridOptions`, scenarios: exact grid lines, impedance within 3 percent across cells 0.07 to 0.019 mm, graded within 1.5 percent in capacitance and 1 percent in impedance of uniform on 20 times fewer nodes). The cell-independent numbers (`[.report]`): Nordic coplanar feed 54.6 to 55.1 ohm as a sheet (closed form 54.6) and 50.0 to 50.7 ohm with 38.5 um of copper (the vendor's 50 ohm intent); the IFA board's feed 58.3 to 58.6 ohm as a sheet (closed form 58) and 54.4 to 54.9 ohm with 35 um. This replaces the 56.75 ohm sheet figure recorded in the 2D-solve section above: that number was a rasterisation artifact (the gap snapped to a whole number of cells), the closed form was right for a sheet, and the thickness effect is 6.6 to 8.4 percent. (1) *GPU kernel: BUILT* (`shaders/line2d_rb.comp`, `fdtd_vk::GpuLine2d`): red-black over-relaxation in float32 over the rasteriser's link conductances, batches of 100 sweeps with the change read back, capacitance summed in double on the CPU; the stopping test also accepts five batches without improvement below 1e-4 (the float noise floor with omega 1.96 sat above 1e-6 and ran all 200000 sweeps before that rule); `[gpu]` scenarios agree with the CPU reference within 0.1 percent in capacitance and 0.5 percent in extrapolated impedance, uniform and graded. *Measured (M1 Pro, release, under the lock):* Nordic feed extrapolated solve, uniform grid: CPU 2.18 s, GPU 0.86 s; graded grid: CPU 0.07 s, GPU 0.38 s (fixed costs of buffers and read-back). So the GPU pays only on large uniform grids, and the window's feed row uses the CPU solver. (4) *Antenna tab feed-solve row: BUILT* (`board/feed_solve.*`, window background thread, cached on the line's numbers; headless screenshots wait for it; `docs/ux.md`). On the IFA test board the closed form says 58 ohm (+17 percent, flagged) and the solve says 54.4 ohm with 35 um copper (+9 percent, within the 10 percent band): the earlier "more than 10 percent off, change the width" advice was the closed form's overestimate. Limits: one measured gap (symmetric side grounds assumed), one averaged permittivity for the dielectric stack, no bends or stubs. Not done: a one-sided-ground flag in `FeedReport`; the solver's own copper thickness (the model keeps sheets).

**Verification of the four items (2026-11-23).** `scripts/check.sh` passes (sanitizer build, CPU tests, formatting, ruff and pytest 26 cases) and so did `--gpu`; the `[slow]` suite passes in release (20 cases, 178 assertions). `line2d_rb.comp` passes its `[gpu][line2d]` scenarios on both drivers (KosmicKrisp and MoltenVK, one run each); timings, one run each so differences of a few percent are not established: Nordic feed extrapolated solve, uniform grid, CPU 2.16 s, GPU 0.52 s (KosmicKrisp) and 0.94 s (MoltenVK); graded grid, CPU 0.06 s, GPU 0.09 s and 0.42 s.
