So the model of TI's own board reproduces the physics (a 50 ohm resonance of about the right width) and misses the frequency by 8 percent. *What can account for 8 percent that the model does not have or sets by assumption:* the dielectric constant (TI states 4.5 with no frequency; FR4 is nearer 4.2 to 4.4 at 2.4 GHz: a lower Dk raises the resonance), solder mask over the copper (lowers it), copper as a sheet (the slab model raises it by about 0.65 percent), the components and chip near the antenna, and the traced copper (0.03 mm). Sensitivity runs for the Dk (4.2) and Dk 4.2 with the slab model are queued; they are sensitivities, not a fit, and the unfitted result above is the headline. *Earlier statements this replaces:* the "short ground behind the arm and 1.0 mm board" configuration that gave -14.9 dB and 2.396 GHz was a fitted guess at a board TI did not use; its agreement with TI's description was coincidental to the extent that its ground and stackup differ from TI's.

**RF engineering skills and Gerber tooling (2026-11-23, requested by the user).** Two project skills: `rf-board-engineering` (the judgement layer: trust order, sizing before modelling, which lever moves which number with its example numbers marked as examples, fab stackups, where each skill and script lives) and `gerber-to-model` (a vendor's manufacturing files into a solver model: licence and untrusted-data handling, reading the readme and every layer, the recipe, the port and its controls, the limits to state). Tracked tooling in `scripts/gerber/` (reader and rasteriser, tracer, recipe builder, self-test in `scripts/check.sh`): it reproduces the TI model built earlier by hand (same outline, port and 58 ground vias; layer 1 has two more shapes because the first rasteriser skipped rotated centre-rectangle pads, macro primitive 21, which the reader now draws; the antenna region is pixel-identical). The recipe and TI's files stay private in `out/antennas/swrr034c/`.

**TI's measured curve read (2026-11-23, `out/antennas/ti_pages/p-10.png`, Figure 2-8 of SWRU120D, read from the plot, about +-0.02 GHz and +-0.5 dB).** Measured S11 at the antenna's feed point: a single dip to **-24 dB at about 2.42 GHz**, S11 below -10 dB from about 2.29 to 2.59 GHz (about 295 MHz), a shoulder at about -10 dB between 2.2 and 2.25 GHz, -5 dB at 2.0 and 3.0 GHz. The model of the same board from its Gerbers (unfitted, `run-20261123-160002`): dip -26.9 dB at 2.236 GHz, below -10 dB from 2.130 to 2.391 GHz (261 MHz). So the depth (-27 against -24 dB) and the bandwidth (261 against about 295 MHz) agree, and the **frequency is 7.6 percent low (2.236 against 2.42 GHz)**; the 2.44 GHz used in the entries above was my approximation of TI's target, not read from the figure, and the figure shows the dip at 2.42. *Sensitivity so far:* Dk 4.5 to 4.2 (`run-20261123-160449`): resonance 2.2445 to 2.2624 GHz (+0.8 percent), best -23.6 dB at 2.252 GHz, 212 MHz below -10 dB, so the dielectric constant is not what closes the gap (a larger Dk change than FR4 allows would be needed); copper as slab: queued with it. What the gap can still be: solder mask (lowers the frequency), the omitted VCC plane, chip and parts near the antenna, and the traced geometry; none is large enough on its own that I have sized. Not a fitted agreement and not a failure: the unfitted model reproduces the antenna's depth and width on TI's own board.

**TI board, sensitivities complete (2026-11-23).** All on `ti_cc2430db.json`, the controlled port, 0.2 mm antenna box; TI's measured curve for reference: dip -24 dB at about 2.42 GHz, about 295 MHz below -10 dB. (1) Unfitted, Dk 4.5, copper as sheets (`run-20261123-160002`): series resonance 2.2445 GHz, R 55.5 ohm, dip -26.9 dB at 2.236 GHz, 261 MHz. (2) Dk 4.2 (`160449`): 2.2624 GHz, R 57.4, dip -23.6 dB at 2.252 GHz, 212 MHz. (3) Dk 4.2 with the slab copper model, 35 um on all four layers (`160910`, 86 624 steps at 0.083 ps, 364 s): series resonance **2.308 GHz**, R 67.3 ohm, dip -17.7 dB at 2.277 GHz (Z = 63.6 - 5.9j ohm), **302 MHz below -10 dB (2.162 to 2.464 GHz)**, 106 MHz below -15 dB, and -11.9 to -9.8 dB across 2.4 to 2.4835 GHz. *Reading:* the slab raises the resonance 2.0 percent here (0.65 percent on the IFA test board) and the resistance by 10 ohm, so copper thickness is not a small correction on this thin-dielectric board; with it and a plausible Dk the model's dip is 2.277 GHz, 5.9 percent below TI's 2.42, still low, now 302 MHz wide against TI's 295 MHz, and shallower (-17.7 against -24 dB) because the resistance rose to 67 ohm. The two sensitivities together close about 4 of the 7.6 percent; solder mask (which lowers the frequency, so it would widen the gap), the omitted VCC plane and the chip's and parts' loading are what remain untested. These are sensitivities of a model, not a fit: the unfitted run stays the headline, and the slab and Dk values are choices, not measurements of TI's board.

**Paywalled papers: abstracts, proofs, reproductions (2026-11-23, requested by the user).** Register and rules in `docs/methods/paper-claims.md`; abstracts came from OpenAlex (Crossref was rate-limited, publisher pages returned nothing), are paraphrased and not stored; no paywall was circumvented. Distinction used: a description of a paper's work is reproduced by measuring the same quantity; an accuracy bound on an unread formula cannot be checked, only the quantity measured; a theorem can be proved. *Results:* (1) **Fano / Bode-Fano**: proved from network theory (`bode-fano.md`: the load capacitor absorbed into the network, |S11| = |S22| of a lossless two-port, the Blaschke product and a Cauchy contour integral; area = pi/(R C_tot) - pi sum(sigma_k) <= pi/(R C)); numerically a bare resistor already has the whole area pi, 3000 random ladders never exceed it, and optimised ladders of 0 to 5 elements reach 28, 55, 69, 77, 82 and 85 percent of pi in-band; an earlier version of the script reached 150 percent by exploiting an under-resolved notch in its integral, not a network. (2) **Hammerstad-Jensen and Simons** static forms against an independent 2D solve: microstrip 21 cases, eeff within 0.28 percent, Z0 +0.10 to +0.53 percent; conductor-backed coplanar 12 cases, eeff 0.0 to 0.2 percent for gap <= h/2 and up to 0.9 percent at gap = h, Z0 +0.3 to +1.0 percent; the 2D solver's walls bias eeff low by 2 percent at 4 widths (3.155, 3.201, 3.215 at 4, 8, 16), which had made the closed form look high. `line_impedance.hpp`'s "coplanar not confirmed" is updated. (3) **The FDTD solver's own effective permittivity** (new finding, `line-extraction-and-bends.md`): by two-length extraction a 2.0 mm line on 1.6 mm of er 4.4 reads eeff 3.561, 3.467, 3.403 at lateral cells of 0.2, 0.15, 0.1 mm against 3.219 quasi-static (10.6, 7.7, 5.7 percent high), independent of vertical resolution (3.401 with 16 cells through the board); the increments of 0.095 and 0.063 for equal 0.05 mm steps give an observed order of about 2.2 and an extrapolated limit of about 3.36, roughly 4 percent above quasi-static and larger than any dispersion at 2 GHz: an **open question about the solver**, to be settled by a pair at 0.05 mm cells (not run, about an hour each); at antenna-model cells a line's phase velocity is about 5 percent slow, in the direction of the coarse-mesh antenna detuning found earlier, but the inverted-F moved only 0.4 percent between 0.2 and 0.1 mm while this line's eeff moved 4.6 percent, which is not understood. (4) **Douville-James bends** measured (not compared with their unread formulas): a square corner on a 2.0 mm line is a 0.17 pF shunt capacitance with a 2.0 mm path shortening; mitring removes the capacitance, which crosses zero at a standard mitre fraction of about 0.70 (reflection -43 dB at the sampled 0.675, against -21.5 dB for a square corner). *Not done:* Kirschning's open-end claim (formula unread; the offset measured lumps the open end, the strip behind the port and the port), Kirschning-Jansen-Koster 1983 (no abstract anywhere), dispersion (the fitted slope has the wrong sign: not confirmed), strip-thickness correction of Hammerstad-Jensen (only sized with the 2D solve). *Method lesson:* three scripts sharing one snapshot file contaminated a batch (a run waiting for the GPU read another run's geometry); runs now write one snapshot per run, results are re-read before being written, and the suspect runs were redone sequentially.

**Feed-row wall margin (2026-11-23).** The Antenna tab's field solve (`board/feed_solve.cpp`) now puts the grounded walls 8 line-widths away instead of 4: at 4 they bias the effective permittivity about 2 percent low (eeff 3.155, 3.201, 3.215 at 4, 8, 16; `docs/methods/microstrip-closed-forms.md`), as large as the copper effect the row exists to show. On the IFA test board's feed with the OSH Park profile's 43 um copper the solve reads 53.8 ohm; figures quoted earlier in this plan (54.4 ohm with 35 um at margin 4) were at the old margin.

**Open-end formulas (2026-11-23).** The 1981 Kirschning-Jansen-Koster open-end extension and Hammerstad's were taken from the Qucs technical documentation (page read in full, equation images read where the text was cut; papers unread) into `scripts/antenna/openend.py`; they agree within 4 percent (0.58 and 0.57 mm at 2.0 mm width). The FDTD end offset with the strip behind the port removed is 1.40 to 1.44 mm: the strip behind the port is additive (+0.46 mm for +0.5 mm), a no-strip case did not fit and is not a result. The roughly 0.8 mm excess mixes the port, the mesh's permittivity excess and the open end, so the 2.5 percent bound is untested. The 1983 digest is identified by title (a resonator/gap paper), abstract not obtained. Next if wanted: a different termination at the same port, or 0.05 mm cells.

**TI board: solder mask, VCC plane, parts and mesh, sized one at a time, then combined (2026-11-23).** All runs on the Gerber-built CC2430DB model, controlled port, `--profile bluetooth --set mesh_preset=coarse --set decay_threshold=0.01`, feed box 0.06 mm, antenna box 0.2 mm unless stated; frequencies are the series resonance (reactance crosses zero), 8.2 MHz resolution (0.37 percent); TI's measured dip is at about 2.42 GHz. Files private in `out/antennas/swrr034c/` (`ti_nol3.json`, `ti_vcc.json`, `ti_vcc_g.json`, `ti_parts.json`, `ti_all.json`, `tools/add_bodies.py`, run scripts and logs). *Controls first:* rebuilding the model with the current Gerber tool (2 extra shapes far from the antenna, rotated pads that the first rasteriser skipped) gives 2.2467 GHz, R 55.2 ohm (old file 2.2445, 55.5); the fine-air settings the body runs need change nothing (2.2441, 55.6). The fresh no-layer-3 model (`run-20261123-...` log `ctl_nol3`) is the baseline below.

| Item | Result against the baseline 2.2467 GHz, R 55.2 | How known |
|---|---|---|
| **VCC plane (layer 3)** floating | 2.2211 GHz (**-1.1 %**), dip -28.9 dB, 220 MHz | FDTD, one run |
| VCC plane tied to the layer-2 ground by 28 vias | 2.2206 GHz (same) | FDTD: the shift does not depend on the plane's potential; the plane's solid copper ends 21 mm below the port and no via pierces it, which is expected: the plane sits 0.34 mm from the layer-2 ground, so it is RF-grounded whatever its DC state, and adding it is a counterpoise change of the kind the ground-crop study found (up to 2 percent); not separately tested |
| **Solder mask**, 20 to 40 um, er 3.3 to 4.2 (assumed: not in TI's readme) | frequency scale **-0.5 to -1.2 %** for a strip over the board with ground 2 mm below it (the radiator), **-1.1 to -2.7 %** for lines over close ground (the feed) | 2D quasi-static solve (`feed_solve_test` "Report: what a solder mask does", hidden), mask taken as a uniform layer over strip and board; TI's `stopcomp` file shows the whole radiator covered, openings only over pads below it. Not run in 3D: a 20 to 40 um layer is under two cells (smallest cell 0.0565 mm) and the voxelizer would need a change |
| **Chip body** (7 x 7 x 0.9 mm, er 4) | 2.2627 GHz (+0.8 %), R 62.5 | FDTD, size and permittivity assumed |
| Chip body as metal (upper bound) | 2.2724 (+1.2 %), R 60.3 | FDTD |
| **Seven small parts** (0.5 mm tall, er 10, 1 to 3 cells wide so poorly resolved) | 2.2730 (+1.2 %), R 60.1; parallel resonance 2.68 to 2.56 GHz | FDTD, crude bracket |
| **Mesh**: antenna box 0.15 mm | 2.2360 (-0.4 %) | FDTD |
| antenna box 0.1 mm | 2.2685 (**+1.0 %**), R 57.8 | FDTD; non-monotonic, so the scatter between meshes is about 1 to 1.5 percent |
| Copper slab and Dk 4.2 (earlier runs) | +2.0 % and +0.8 % | FDTD |

*Combined run* (`combined.log`: layer 3, chip er 4 and small parts er 10, Dk 4.2 on all three dielectrics, slab copper, antenna box 0.1 mm, 7.9 M cells, smallest cell 0.035 mm): series resonance **2.375 GHz (+5.7 %)**, R **77.8 ohm**, dip -13.9 dB at 2.309 GHz (Z 73 - 9j), **351 MHz** below -10 dB (2.154 to 2.505 GHz). The effects do not simply add (the individual shifts sum to about +4.6 percent without the mask, the combination gave +5.7). *Mask applied by hand* (not in the run): -0.8 to -1.2 percent on the radiator takes the series resonance to about 2.35 GHz, **about 3 percent below TI's 2.42 GHz** (the dip, 2.309 GHz, is 4.6 percent below). *Reading, plainly:* (1) the frequency gap shrank from 7.6 percent to roughly 3 to 5, with every added effect pointing the way physics says and none large alone; the two biggest (copper thickness and parts) rest on assumed values (35 um from a readme typo "0.35um", package size and permittivity). (2) **The resistance and the depth got worse**: R 78 ohm and -13.9 dB against TI's 50 ohm and -24 dB, so the model is closer in frequency and further in match; a model whose frequency improved by adding assumed parts is not thereby more right. (3) The mesh result matters for the earlier conclusion: the 0.2 mm antenna cells that were "converged to 0.4 percent" on the IFA test board scatter by 1 to 1.5 percent on this antenna; the line study predicts a bias that falls with the cell (about 2 percent low at 0.2 mm, 1 percent at 0.1 mm if the antenna follows 40 percent of a line's sensitivity, the ratio seen between 0.2 and 0.1 mm here), plus the unexplained 4 percent above quasi-static in the limit. That is an inference from non-monotonic data (0.15 mm fell, 0.1 mm rose) and is withdrawn until the mesh scatter is understood (see the iteration below). *Still open after this:* the 4 percent line-permittivity excess in the limit; what sets R (the extra 20 ohm: copper loss, parts loading and mask all enter); TI's measurement conditions (cable, balun cut, reference plane); loss tangent (0.02 assumed); the 279 signal-via barrels left out; a 3D mask. *Next, if wanted:* a mask model in the voxelizer with a thin-layer rule, a run at 0.075 mm cells, TI board with parts changes one at a time at the finer mesh.
*Iteration results (TI board, no layer 3, slab copper; series resonance, R).* **Mesh (antenna box cell)**: 0.2 mm 2.2467 GHz/55 ohm, 0.15 2.2360/58, 0.1 2.2685/58 (2.2796/63 with the box shifted 0.05 mm: grid-phase scatter about 0.5 percent and 5 ohm), 0.075 2.3026/64, 0.05 (14 M cells) 2.3270/66. The sequence is **not converged**: steps of +1.5 percent (0.1 to 0.075) and +1.1 percent (0.075 to 0.05); extrapolating linearly in the cell gives about 2.37 GHz (plus or minus 0.03) and R about 68 ohm. So the 0.2 mm result is about 5 percent low and the 0.1 mm result 4 percent low on this antenna: the earlier 'converged to 0.4 percent' claim holds only for the IFA test board. **Copper slab thickness** (0.2 mm antenna cells): 35 um 2.2806, 17.5 um 2.2748, 5 um 2.2715 GHz (R 59.6/59.8/60.0): the slab effect is physical (a thickness-independent shift, not a discretisation artefact), and the 5 um run (dt 0.0157 ps, 457 k steps, 31 min) settles within 0.4 percent of 35 um. **Decay stop**: 0.01 vs 0.001 changes the 0.2 mm baseline negligibly; dec4_c02 hit max_steps (200000) and is not a clean point (2.2447). *Where this leaves the model.* The mesh drift (+5 percent from 0.2 to the limit) is larger than any single physical term and has the same sign as TI's offset: with the mesh limit about 2.37 GHz, the remaining distance to TI's 2.42 GHz (about 2 percent, before the hand-applied mask of -0.5 to -1.2 percent) is inside the uncertainty of the assumed inputs (copper 35 um from a readme typo, chip and parts permittivity, loss tangent 0.02, measurement reference plane). It is not a demonstrated agreement. R stays 15 to 20 ohm above TI's 50 ohm at every mesh, so the match depth is not trusted. *What a designer can trust today:* the centre frequency to about plus or minus 5 percent at 0.1 mm cells (low bias, so tune up), better after a 0.05 mm check or the extrapolation; the -10 dB bandwidth (about 270 to 300 MHz, TI 295) to about 10 percent; the dip depth and R not at all (grid-phase noise 5 ohm, bias 10 to 20 ohm). *Solver issues named:* non-monotonic, first-order-looking mesh convergence of the antenna frequency (staircased 0.25 to 0.45 mm features); the line eeff excess; the R offset. *Next:* align cell edges to the radiator's feature widths or add conformal treatment (patent check first) to cut the drift; parts one at a time at the 0.05 mm mesh.
*Solver issues, resolved (2026-11-24; supersedes the mesh conclusions of the iteration above).* **1. The mesh drift was a missing snap, not curved metal.** `snapToGeometry` only used copper shapes of 8 vertices or fewer, and the Gerber trace merges the radiator into a pour of 4288 vertices, so the radiator's 0.25 to 0.45 mm traces were never on grid lines and their width was off by up to a cell. The radiator itself is axis-aligned (91 vertices in its window, 5 short non-axis edges), so no conformal scheme would have helped. Fix: inside a refine box, the long straight edges (at least 2 cells, 0.15 mm minimum) of any copper shape become grid lines (`src/board/voxelize.cpp`; scenario in `tests/cpu/board_test.cpp`). **2. Lateral growth ratio** is now a setting (`lateral_growth_ratio`, default 1.3, the old fixed value, so earlier results reproduce). Snapped TI series, series resonance: ratio 1.3: 0.2 mm 2.2771, 0.1 mm 2.2776, 0.1 mm box shifted 0.05 mm 2.3071, 0.075 mm 2.3221 GHz (scatter about 1.3 percent); ratio 1.1: 0.2 mm 2.2816, 0.1 mm 2.2773, shifted 2.2793, 0.075 mm 2.3015, 0.05 mm 2.3076 GHz (scatter 0.2 percent at 0.2 to 0.1, last step 0.3 percent); ratio 1.05: 0.2 mm 2.2632, 0.1 mm 2.2932 (a 1.3 percent spread, so smoother is not monotonically better). **The base model (sheet copper, no layer 3, no parts) reads 2.28 to 2.31 GHz, R about 61 ohm, -10 dB band 261 to 277 MHz**: two plateaus, 2.2816/2.2773 at 0.2/0.1 mm and 2.3015/2.3076 at 0.075/0.05 mm, with an unexplained 1.1 percent jump between 0.1 and 0.075 mm (the last step alone is 0.3 percent, which is not the same as converged). It is not the 2.37 GHz extrapolated from the unsnapped series. Use ratio 1.1 for antenna boxes. **3. The 'line permittivity excess' (3.40 against 3.219) was mostly the extraction on a wide board, not the solver.** An air-filled 2 mm line must read eeff 1.000: on the 60 by 30 mm board it read 1.040 (fit residual 0.0065) at every lateral growth ratio (1.3, 1.1, 1.05) and with fine air cells; on a 44 by 14 mm board it reads 1.0000 plus or minus 0.002 (residual 0.0024), with the whole board on one 0.1 mm cell (snapped or not) and with the narrow box alike. So the size of the ground plane, not the grid, biases the two-length fit (board-edge modes the straight-line model lacks). On the 44 by 14 mm board the er 4.4 line reads eeff 3.378, 3.2975, 3.2678 at 0.2, 0.1, 0.05 mm cells (Z0 63.9, 63.6, 63.5 ohm; closed form 3.219 plus about 0.012 of dispersion, Z0 63.5): the steps shrink by 2.7, so the limit is about 3.25, **within about 0.6 percent of quasi-static**. er 2.2 on the wide board read 1.901 against 1.79, so the wide-board bias is general; a line study needs a ground plane only a few strip heights wider than the line. The earlier methods note's 5.7 percent figure and its 'tension' with the antenna are withdrawn accordingly (see `docs/methods/line-extraction-and-bends.md`). **4. Slab copper is physical**: 35, 17.5 and 5 um give 2.2806, 2.2748, 2.2715 GHz. 

*Ledger for the TI CC2430DB model (2026-11-24, end of this iteration).* Combined model (slab copper, Dk 4.2, layer 3, parts as dielectric bodies, ratio 1.1, box refined) series resonance against cell: 0.2 mm 2.3669 GHz (R 68.6 ohm), 0.075 mm 2.4043 (R 71.9), 0.05 mm 2.4465 (R 74.9, dip -14.1 dB, band 375 MHz). Loss tangent 0.01, 0.02, 0.03 at 0.2 mm: R 67.3, 68.6, 69.4 ohm, frequency unchanged (2.367 GHz), so loss does not set R. Terms at 0.2 mm (non-additive): slab +4.1 percent, Dk 4.2 against 4.5 +2.1, layer 3 +0.6, parts -0.7. **These are not clean physics terms.** The removal runs are not on the same grid: f_noslab_02 has 80 z cells and smallest cell 0.0456 mm, the others 83 and 0.035 mm (dt 0.0987 against 0.0767 ps), so each removal is a material change plus a remesh. Sizes quoted for the slab disagree by context: about 0 in the base (slab 35 um 2.2806 GHz against sheet 2.2816, from earlier ratio-1.3 nol3 runs, with a 0.4 percent thickness trend from 35 to 5 um), +4.1 in the combined model. Honest term sizes need the same grid in both arms (a sheet run on the slab grid). TI measured 2.42 GHz, 50 ohm, -24 dB, 295 MHz. **Verdict: not converged, and the stopping criterion is not met.** The combined model keeps drifting up with finer cells (+1.6 percent from 0.2 to 0.075, +1.8 percent more to 0.05 mm, not shrinking), unlike the base model (0.3 percent last step). The agreement with TI's 2.42 GHz at 0.075 mm is therefore a coincidence of an unconverged mesh, not a result: no frequency band can be quoted yet for the combined model. Suspects: slab edge faces and the small dielectric bodies (P1 and P2 are 10 x 1 x 6 cells, under two cells in one direction); Dk is a uniform scalar and layer 3 a sheet, so neither can drift with the mesh. dt barely changes between 0.2 and 0.075 mm (0.0767 against 0.0776 ps; the 0.06 mm feed box sets the smallest cell, and the port stays inside that fixed box, to be confirmed), so the port discretisation is not the drift. Next, one series: base plus slab at 0.2/0.1/0.05 mm; if it drifts, the slab is the cause, if not, the bodies. What a designer can trust today: the base (sheet) model at about 2.31 GHz with 0.3 to 0.5 percent mesh scatter; trends between variants (length, keepout) rather than absolute frequency, ~2 percent at best; R and the dip depth (R 61 to 75 ohm against TI 50) not at all. **Slab series result (base model, ratio 1.1, slab against sheet at the same cells):** slab 2.3032, 2.3205, 2.3268 GHz at 0.2, 0.1, 0.05 mm (steps +0.75 then +0.27 percent, shrinking by 2.8, limit about 2.33), R 63.4, 62.9, 62.4 ohm; sheet 2.2816, 2.2773, 2.3076. So the slab converges and is not the cause of the combined model's drift; its size in the base context is about +0.8 to +0.9 percent at 0.2 and 0.05 mm (the earlier +4.1 percent belonged to the combined model and a changed grid: the slab grid has 73 z cells against 62). Remaining suspects for the +1.6 and +1.8 percent steps of the combined model: the dielectric bodies (P1 and P2 under two cells across), layer 3, and the air-near settings used only in that model. **Bodies series result (base model, sheet copper, bodies as dielectric, ratio 1.1):** 2.2753, 2.2727, 2.3053 GHz at 0.2, 0.1, 0.05 mm (R 56.5, 56.2, 64.2) against sheet 2.2816, 2.2773, 2.3076: the bodies shift it only -0.3, -0.2, -0.1 percent and follow the same path, so they do not drift either. The sheet series' own bump at 0.05 mm (+1.3 percent) coincides with the smallest cell changing (0.0478, 0.0474, 0.0375 mm) while the slab runs hold it at 0.035 mm and read smoothly: the z grid does not change there (62 cells in all three sheet runs), so the bump is lateral, plausibly the snap taper (0.6 neighbour floor) or the antenna box overtaking the 0.06 mm feed box at 0.05 mm; the sheet series is non-monotonic (and its R moves 51.5, 57.1, 61.1 ohm), so it is not in the asymptotic range and is a screening model, while the slab series (steps shrinking by 2.8) is the one to quote. A cheap discriminator is the sheet at 0.1 mm with the feed box also at 0.05 mm. The combined model uses the slab, so its z grid is fixed, yet it still drifts: the remaining suspects are layer 3 and the air-near settings (air_near_cell 0.1, height 1.0) used only there. Ranking after review (2026-11-24): (1) air-near 0.1 mm over the 0.035 mm slab freezes the vertical cell above the copper while the lateral cell is refined, which does not converge, and fits the flat slab R against the drifting combined R; confirm by the a_ series and by comparing the z cells above F.Cu in a_02 and s_02 (the combined model's z count is 83 at all three cells, so no second grid change there); (2) if it reproduces, run the combined model without air-near at 0.2/0.1/0.05 mm (expected converged value about 2.39 GHz before the mask, 2.36 to 2.38 after, against TI 2.42, inside the Dk uncertainty); (3) conformal PEC is parked: the slab edge faces, the one place it would act, converge monotonically (step ratio 2.8), and the radiator is axis-aligned; (4) for R: count the Gerber vias kept against dropped near the feed (279 dropped), and note that TI's 50 ohm is implied by a return-loss dip (-24 dB means |Z-50| under about 6 ohm), so the target is 47 to 53 ohm at the dip, not R = 50, and SWRA117 should be checked for a reference plane. **Air-near result (base model, slab, air_near_cell 0.1, height 1.0, ratio 1.1):** 2.3046, 2.3223, 2.3296 GHz at 0.2, 0.1, 0.05 mm (R 63.2, 62.8, 62.4), against 2.3032, 2.3205, 2.3268 without air-near: no effect (0.1 percent), so the air-near hypothesis is refuted and the base slab series converges (steps +0.77 then +0.31 percent, limit about 2.335). The combined model's extra drift (+1.6 then +1.8 percent) therefore comes from Dk 4.2, layer 3, or the bodies together with the slab; removal runs at 0.075 mm (no Dk change, no layer 3, no parts) are queued to find which. Then check the TI reference plane and mask. Alternatives to a conformal scheme, listed for later review (no DOI cited yet; each must be verified and read before use): feature-aligned grid and polygon snap (done), Richardson extrapolation as a band, dielectric-interface permittivity averaging, edge-length-only conformal correction, thin-strip edge corrections. *Plan: what else could be accelerated or parallelised (2026-11-24; to do later, nothing started).* The solve is bandwidth-bound (182 GB/s measured copy bandwidth, runs at about 1.0 to 1.1 Gcells/s), so offloading CPU stages does not speed it; gains come from fewer bytes per cell-step or fewer steps. Step 0, measure first: time setup (snapshot read, voxelize, grid), solve and post-processing separately on one long run (the CPU phases are not timed today; openEMS set up a similar 4.35 M-cell grid in 80 s), and compare the step kernel's achieved bytes per second with the copy benchmark to confirm it sits at the ceiling. Then, in order of expected payoff and with a scenario and a measurement before each: (1) fewer steps: the 35 um slab cell sets dt (about 30 percent more steps than sheet copper), so a thin-sheet correction, local time stepping or subgridding; this touches the conformal/subcell patent question and waits for the qualified review; (2) fewer bytes: compact material indices with a lookup table instead of per-cell coefficients if that is how they are stored (check first), fp16 field storage only if accuracy tests allow, temporal blocking only if measured to help on a graded grid; (3) far-field transform (`src/fdtd/ntff.cpp`) on the GPU, reusing the phasor accumulator for the surface sums, if far-field runs prove slow; (4) voxelization on the GPU only if step 0 shows setup is a large share; (5) independent small runs (line studies, cropped boards) queued concurrently, which needs a change to the one-run-at-a-time GPU lock policy and so the user's decision; (6) precision: a double-precision CPU run of a cropped board against the GPU to rule fp32 in or out of the combined-model drift, and a check that the Kahan phasor sum holds under both drivers with `MVK_CONFIG_FAST_MATH_ENABLED` on and off (test added in `tests/gpu/gpu_solver_test.cpp`, runs queued). Not worth moving: Gerber tracing (one-off), S11 post-processing, the 2D feed solve (already has a GPU version).

*Removal runs at 0.075 mm, decay-threshold check, openEMS cross-check, Kahan test (2026-11-24, one run each; resolution 8.2 MHz).* **Removal runs** (slab copper, ratio 1.1, antenna box 0.075 mm, feed box 0.06 mm, decay 0.01; "Dk" is `diel1..3_epsilon_r=4.2` against the board's own value, "bodies" is `component_bodies=dielectric`, eps 10, U1 eps 4; layer 3 is the `ti_parts.json` snapshot against `ti_all.json`): series resonance, R at 0.2 then 0.075 mm: full model 2.3669 (68.6) then 2.4043 GHz (71.9), +1.6 percent; no Dk 2.3171 (61.5) then 2.3099 (60.4), -0.3 percent; no bodies 2.3836 (70.3) then 2.3870 (71.3), +0.1 percent; no layer 3 2.3527 (70.9) then 2.4081 (71.9), +2.4 percent. So the drift disappears when either the Dk 4.2 override or the bodies are taken out, and stays when layer 3 is taken out: layer 3 is not the cause, and the drift needs the Dk override and the bodies together. The Dk term itself grows with the mesh (+50 MHz at 0.2 mm, +94 MHz at 0.075 mm), which a uniform scalar should not do: this points at an interaction between the Dk change and the dielectric bodies (the bodies' eps 10 is set relative to the board). It is a hypothesis, not a finding. Grid check: all four variants have 83 z cells and a 0.035 mm smallest cell at both meshes, so the z grid is not a confound here (unlike the slab terms); the x and y cell counts differ by one cell in the no-Dk variant (301 x 218 against 302 x 217 at 0.2 mm, 521 against 522 at 0.075 mm) and the time step moves with Dk (0.0767 to 0.0779 ps), so a one-cell lateral remesh remains in that arm. Snapshot check: `ti_parts.json` differs from `ti_all.json` by 88 copper shapes, 86 on In2.Cu (layer 3, emptied; the 35 um slab layer stays in the stackup) and 2 on F.Cu, so the no-layer-3 arm also drops two top-layer shapes; vias are the same (58). No 0.05 mm removal run existed when this was written (no Dk and no bodies at 0.05 mm are queued, `run_fin4.sh`), the sheet arm was not run on this grid, and the interaction was not tested directly (for example bodies with the board's own Dk). **0.05 mm removal runs (same night, `run_fin4.sh`, 21 M cells, 0.035 mm smallest cell, time step 0.0689 and 0.0729 ps) overturn the Dk reading.** No Dk (bodies kept): 2.3171, 2.3099, 2.3539 GHz at 0.2, 0.075, 0.05 mm (R 61.5, 60.4, 69.4 ohm; dip -17.0 dB), +1.9 percent in the last step, so it is not converged: the 'no Dk' arm only looked converged because it had two meshes. No bodies (Dk 4.2 kept): 2.3836, 2.3870, 2.3922 GHz (R 70.3, 71.3, 72.7 ohm; dip -15.55 dB), +0.14 and +0.22 percent per step, converged within 0.3 percent. Full model: 2.3669, 2.4043, 2.4465. So the drift needs the dielectric component bodies and does not need Dk 4.2 (it is not an interaction of the two); the bodies are the suspect already named in the ledger (P1 and P2 are 10 x 1 x 6 cells, under two cells in one direction). Not tested: bodies on the base model alone at 0.05 mm, a finer vertical cell in the air near the board (`air_near_cell_mm` was 0.1 in these runs), body eps 10 against a lower value. The R at 0.05 mm without bodies, 72.7 ohm, is not near TI's 50 ohm either. R stays 60 to 72 ohm in every variant, so neither the bodies nor Dk removes the gap to TI's 50 ohm. **Decay threshold:** base slab model at 0.2 mm (the `s_02` run, `ti_nol3.json`) rerun at the default `decay_threshold=0.001` instead of 0.01 (`sd_02`, 99 344 steps, 507 s): series resonance 2.3032 to 2.3034 GHz (+0.009 percent), R 63.4 to 61.9 ohm (-1.5 ohm, -2.4 percent), dip -21.15 dB at 2.2766 GHz. The ladder frequencies stand; R values from the 0.01 runs carry about 2 ohm of stop-rule uncertainty. Only the 0.2 mm case was checked. **openEMS cross-check (clean-room, black box, `scripts/antenna/openems_case.py`).** First run (`r02`, on `ti_nol3.json`) was invalid and stopped after 238 556 steps: the mesh had a micro-cell (time step 8.0 fs against 77 fs in our solver, from snapped edge lines a few microns from uniform lines), so the Gaussian pulse lasted 594 376 steps against a cap of 600 000; its energy rose from 5e-19 to 4e-14 because the source was still injecting, not from an instability (no evidence of one), and it could never reach the stop criterion. Fixes: lines closer than half the finest cell are merged (smallest cell now 0.032 mm, time step 62 fs, pulse 76 582 steps), air margin 32 mm (about a quarter wavelength, as the openEMS docs ask for the PML), growth ratio 1.1 as in `s_02`, end criterion 1e-4, and a check that prints every copper shape spanning no mesh line: 85 of 506 shapes, all 0.5 to 1.5 mm traces in the 2 mm coarse region at y 60 to 107 mm, none in the antenna box (openEMS would drop them; our voxelizer does not, a model difference expected to be small, not measured). Second run (`r04`, 8.9 M cells, 16 to 31 steps per second) decayed normally at first (10.8 dB below its peak at step 100 868, 17.8 at 140 948) and then slowly (18.9 at 160 988, 1.1 dB in 20 000 steps), so it would have reached the 600 000-step cap; it was stopped at about 165 000 steps (10.4 ns) and S11 was computed from the port time series already on disk (`--post-only`). **Result, 0.2 mm cells, energy 19 dB below its peak (truncated ring-down):** deepest dip -24.0 dB at 2.2325 GHz (Z 55.6 - 3.6j ohm), series resonance 2.2392 GHz, R 57.7 ohm; truncating the series at 8 ns instead gives 2.2426 GHz, R 62.3 ohm, dip -20.75 dB. So the frequency is stable to about 0.15 percent under truncation, R and the dip depth only to about 5 ohm and 3 dB. Our solver on the same model (`sd_02`, 0.2 mm): 2.3034 GHz, R 61.9 ohm, dip -21.15 dB at 2.2766. The two solvers agree on R (58 to 62 ohm against 62) and differ by about 2.8 percent in series resonance (2.24 against 2.30, 3.9 percent against our ladder limit of about 2.33); neither is mesh-converged here (openEMS at one cell size), the 85 dropped traces and the unmatched settings listed below are untested explanations, and the -10 dB bandwidth is not comparable (112 to 130 MHz in openEMS from the truncated series against 367 MHz). Reading (first version, superseded below): R agreed within the truncation spread at 0.2 mm. **Superseded by the 0.1 mm run:** see the next paragraph; the codes do not agree in R once openEMS is refined. The comparison target is the `s_02`/`sd_02` base model (slab, vias, no bodies, 2.303 GHz, R 62 to 63 ohm at 0.2 mm; ladder limit about 2.33 GHz), not the combined model: `ti_nol3.json` is what the slab ladder ran on. Settings that differ between the two solvers and were not matched (not shown to matter): source bandwidth, stop rule (port voltage in ours, total energy in openEMS), dielectric loss as a constant conductivity at 2.4 GHz, Courant factor, 401 against 101 frequency points. **Kahan accumulator under fast math:** the scenario "The GPU phasor accumulator keeps its precision over many samples" (20 000 accumulates of 1e-3 on one channel, expected within 1e-5 of 20000 x float(1e-3)) passed on KosmicKrisp and MoltenVK, each with `MVK_CONFIG_FAST_MATH_ENABLED` at its default, 0 and 1 (six runs, 9 assertions each). Fast math on did not break it on this machine. I did not run a naive-sum control, so the scenario's power to detect lost compensation is not shown; the stronger scenario in item 13 (1e8 then 100 000 x 1) is still the better test. **Stronger scenario added and run:** "The GPU phasor accumulator does not lose small samples added to a large sum" in `tests/gpu/gpu_solver_test.cpp` (one sample of 1e8 then 30 000 of 1, expected 1e8 + 30 000 within 1e-6; a plain fp32 sum stays at 1e8, error 3e-4) passed on KosmicKrisp and MoltenVK with fast math at its default, one run each. No naive-sum control was run, so that a plain sum fails it is derived (spacing of fp32 at 1e8 is 8), not measured.

*openEMS at 0.1 mm and the inter-code gap (2026-11-25, same snapshot `ti_nol3.json`, slab copper, no bodies).* openEMS 0.1 mm cells (16.1 M cells, smallest cell 0.030 mm, `r05`), S11 from the port series up to 9.49 ns (the run continued to its 220 000-step cap; the extraction used the saved port data, `--post-only`): series resonance 2.2349 GHz, R 53.5 ohm, dip -29.5 dB at 2.2325 GHz (Z 53.3 - 1.0j). Truncating the series at 8.5 ns gives 2.2326 GHz, R 52.8; at 7 ns 2.2222, R 52.1: frequency drifts 0.5 percent and R 1.5 ohm over that range, so quote 2.23 to 2.235 GHz and R 53 +- 1.5 ohm. **Full series (the run reached its 220 000-step cap, 13.95 ns, energy 19.4 dB below peak; the first extraction failed on a relative path after the run, fixed by an absolute output directory in `openems_case.py`, port data intact):** dip -29.7 dB at 2.2375 GHz (Z 53.3 - 0.4j), series resonance 2.2384 GHz, R 53.4 ohm, -10 dB band 2.125 to 2.295 GHz (170 MHz); against the 9.5 ns cut 2.2349 and 53.5, so the cut was within 0.2 percent and 0.1 ohm of the full series. Between 7 ns and 13.95 ns the frequency moves 0.7 percent and R 1.3 ohm. openEMS's frequency is unchanged within 0.2 percent between 0.2 mm (2.2392, 10.4 ns series) and 0.1 mm (2.2384). Energy note: after falling to 13.9 dB below peak (step 127 000) the openEMS energy readout rose to 11.6 dB (step 149 000); the docs say the readout is a rough estimate that ignores materials, so this is not by itself an instability, but the extraction above is the result to use unless the full-length series disagrees by more than the truncation spread. **Comparison.** openEMS: 2.239 GHz (0.2 mm), 2.235 (0.1 mm); R 58 then 53. Our solver on the same snapshot: 2.303, 2.3205, 2.3268 GHz at 0.2, 0.1, 0.05 mm, R about 62 to 63. The gap at comparable fine meshes is about 4 percent in frequency and 9 ohm in R, and the two codes move in opposite directions with refinement, so it does not shrink with the antenna-box cell: it is in geometry or port modelling at a coarser scale (the 85 traces openEMS drops, the coarse mesh, the port through the inner layer). Our R (62) is therefore not confirmed by an independent code; openEMS's 53 ohm is closer to TI's 50 and its 2.235 GHz is farther from TI's 2.42 than ours. What can be said now (revised, see the dropped-trace test below): the 4 percent was mostly a geometry difference, because openEMS's 2 mm coarse mesh drops 85 real copper shapes that our 4 to 5 mm coarse mesh keeps; it is not a solver difference. On matched geometry (`nd_02` against `r04`, both 0.2 mm cells) the two codes put the |S11| minimum within 0.2 percent (2.2277 against 2.2325 GHz); what they disagree on is the impedance there (ours 64.4 - 10.9j, openEMS 53 - 0j or so) and the shape of Z(f) (our zero crossing sits 25 to 30 MHz above the dip, openEMS's 1 to 7 MHz). That roughly 10 ohm is the open inter-code item. **Body runs at 0.05 mm (full model, Dk 4.2, slab):** body eps 4 gives 2.4508 GHz, R 75.4 ohm, dip -13.9 dB (settings.json confirms eps 4.0, so the later `--set` wins); eps 10 gave 2.4465. A shift that does not follow the body permittivity, and changes sign with the mesh (bodies lower the resonance 0.7 percent at 0.2 mm and raise it 2.4 percent at 0.05 mm), looks like a mesh artefact of the geometry snapping around the bodies, not dielectric loading: tested (see next).

*Coarse-cell test, matched geometry (2026-11-25, `nd_med_02`, `nd_med_01`).* Same snapshot (`ti_nol3_nodrop.json`), slab copper, decay 0.001 and refine boxes as `nd_02`/`nd_01`, only `mesh_preset=medium` (20 cells per wavelength, coarse cell halved). 0.2 mm: 5.5 M cells, 99 776 steps, 499 s; dip -20.5 dB at 2.3827 GHz (Z 60.4 - 0.8j), series resonance 2.3859 GHz, R 60.5 ohm. 0.1 mm: 9.5 M cells, 117 952 steps, 906 s; dip -32.8 dB at 2.3746 GHz (Z 48.2 + 1.3j), series resonance 2.3714 GHz, R 47.9 ohm, -10 dB band 2.293 to 2.464 GHz (171 MHz). Z at 2.20/2.25/2.30/2.35/2.40 GHz: 0.2 mm 63.1-34.4j, 61.5-27.6j, 59.8-18.9j, 59.4-8.4j, 61.3+3.2j; 0.1 mm 73.2-39.2j, 62.7-42.2j, 49.9-28.9j, 46.6-9.0j, 51.8+11.4j. **Result:** with the coarse cell halved the matched-geometry dip is mesh-stable between 0.2 and 0.1 mm (2.3827, 2.3746 GHz, 0.3 percent) where the coarse-preset runs jumped from 2.2277 to 2.4888 GHz. So the earlier non-convergence of the matched geometry was the coarse cell (or its interaction with the refine box), not the antenna physics. The medium 0.1 mm run is also the nearest to the 50 ohm and -24 dB figures of TI (R 48 ohm, dip -33 dB, though the depth of a dip this sharp depends on the 8 MHz frequency resolution) and its reactance slope near resonance rises about 13 ohm per 50 MHz at 2.30 to 2.35 GHz, against 5 ohm for the coarse preset and about 31 for openEMS. Still open: R changes 12 ohm between the two cell sizes (60.5 to 47.9) so R is not converged at medium; the dip is 6 percent above openEMS's 2.2375 GHz on the same geometry, so the inter-code frequency gap has reappeared, now at a mesh where ours is stable; every earlier number in this ledger that used the coarse preset (the 0.2/0.1/0.05 ladder at 2.303 to 2.327 GHz, R about 62, the body runs, the 'converged base' estimate) must be treated as coarse-cell dependent until rerun at medium. The body-drift conclusion above (grid-line placement) is not yet separated from this. Next: medium preset on the full model and on the no-bodies model at 0.1 mm, then fine preset on matched geometry to see if R and the frequency settle.

*Fine preset, matched geometry, 0.1 mm (2026-11-25, `nd_fine_01`, 11.6 M cells, 133 664 steps, 1313 s).* Dip -29.8 dB at 2.3093 GHz (Z 52.3 - 2.4j); Z at 2.20/2.25/2.30/2.35/2.40/2.45 GHz: 47.0-38.1j, 46.1-21.1j, 51.0-4.8j, 59.8+7.4j, 67.4+15.9j, 74.3+27.9j. Dip against the coarse-cell preset at 0.1 mm: coarse 2.4888, medium 2.3746, fine 2.3093 GHz, so the frequency has not converged in the coarse cell (-2.8 percent from medium to fine, same sign as the step before it) and sits above openEMS's 2.2375 by 3.2 percent; the reactance slope near the dip is now about +16 ohm per 50 MHz (2.30 to 2.35 GHz) against 13 (medium), 5 (coarse) and about 31 (openEMS). R at the dip: 48 (medium), 52 (fine). So the matched geometry moves toward openEMS as the coarse cell shrinks, and the earlier 'inter-code gap' was largely the coarse mesh; whether it closes is not yet known (next: cells_per_wavelength 40 at 0.1 mm). No frequency from the coarse preset is converged, including the TI-vs-model comparison.

*Medium-and-finer batch (2026-11-25; all 0.1 mm antenna box, slab copper, decay 0.001, refine boxes as `nd_01`; logs `out/antennas/swrr034c/*.log`).* **Mesh scatter (provisional: one discriminating run pending).**  Matched geometry (`ti_nol3_nodrop`) dip against the coarse cell: 20 cells per wavelength (medium) 2.3746 GHz, 30 (fine) 2.3093, 45 2.3419 (-24.5 dB, Z 55.9-2.3j, 15.8 M cells, 1313 s); with the 0.2 mm fine air layer 2.3827 (and 2.3991 against 2.3827 at 0.2 mm). Beyond medium the dip does not move in one direction: scatter about +-1.5 percent (2.309 to 2.375), so provisionally quote frequency to +-1.5 percent from this method on this board; the z-cell count also changed between these runs (79, 89, 105), so cells-per-wavelength changed more than the lateral coarse cell, and a 60 cells-per-wavelength point with a grid-statistics diff decides whether this is grid-phase scatter or an unconverged coarse region; R 48 to 56 ohm across those runs (+-4 ohm). The fine air layer (0.2 mm cell, 3 mm high) is worth +0.3 to +0.7 percent: not the cause of the R or frequency spread. **Full geometry (87 shapes kept, no layer 3): `sd_med_01`** medium 0.1 mm, dip -25.2 dB at 2.2930 GHz, Z 55.1-2.7j, Z(2.20..2.45) = 45.6-27.0j, 50.1-13.0j, 56.3-0.8j, 63.8+9.7j, 72.1+19.1j, 82.1+28.5j. **Full model with layer 3, no bodies (`all_nob_med_01`, Dk 4.2): dip -18.3 dB at 2.3909 GHz, Z 63.8+0.3j; with air bodies (eps 1; `all_b1_med_01`): -18.6 dB at 2.4154 GHz, Z 63.3+0.9j.** So the grid-line shift from body corners moves the dip +1.0 percent at medium (inside the scatter), where at the coarse preset and 0.05 mm it looked like +3 percent; the body-drift conclusion from the coarse runs does not stand as a physics finding, and its mechanism is confirmed in the code: `voxelize.cpp` adds every modelled body's corners (up to 12 vertices) as global x and y snap lines, and a dry run with eps-1 bodies has the same cell counts but a different time step (0.0729 to 0.0692 ps at 0.05 mm). **Correction (same day, found by reading the run flags): `all_nob_med_01` and `all_b1_med_01` used `--set diel1/2/3_epsilon_r=4.2`, while `sd_med_01` and every `nd_*` run use the snapshot's 4.5 (TI's readme).** So the step from `sd_med_01` (`ti_nol3`, Dk 4.5) 2.2930 GHz, R 55, to `all_nob_med_01` (`ti_all`, Dk 4.2) 2.3909 GHz, R 64 (+4.3 percent) mixes layer 3 with a 6.7 percent change in Dk; it is not a layer-3 effect, and the comparison with TI's 2.42 GHz below is for an assumed Dk of 4.2, not TI's stated 4.5. Only two In2.Cu shapes lie near the antenna (x 49.5 to 52.1, y 118.5 to 121.0 mm, 3 mm from the port, and x 71.6 to 74.2, y 118.5 to 121.1, beyond the radiator's end); the other 84 are elsewhere on the board. The 2 by 2 that separates the two factors (`ti_nol3` and `ti_all`, each at Dk 4.5 and 4.2, medium, 0.1 mm) is `sd_med_01`, `all_nodk_med_01` (queued), `sd_dk42_med_01` (queued) and `all_nob_med_01`. Both runs keep the 87 small shapes that openEMS drops (those are F.Cu, B.Cu and In1.Cu, a different set). The 87 shapes' own effect has changed sign with the mesh: removing them lowered the dip 2.2 percent at the coarse preset and 0.2 mm (2.2766 to 2.2277) and raised it 3.6 percent at medium and 0.1 mm (`sd_med_01` 2.2930 to `nd_med_01` 2.3746); the coarse 0.2 mm pair is not evidence and is dropped from the ledger. **Against TI at medium (full model, layer 3):** no bodies 2.3909, air bodies 2.4154 GHz, against TI's 2.42 read from the figure: 1.2 and 0.2 percent below, inside the +-1.5 percent scatter; the frequency gap to TI is not resolved as a defect. What is left is R (about 64 ohm against about 50) and depth (-18 dB against -24); the source of the extra ohms (layer 3, Dk 4.2, or neither) is the 2 by 2 above. **Microstrip controls (50 ohm line, 1.6 mm, er 4.4, lossless, 30 mm open line, closed form 50.0 ohm, eps_eff 3.33):** fitted Z0 50.8 (coarse preset), 50.8 (medium), 50.4 (fine) ohm, i.e. +0.8 to +1.6 percent; sheet copper. openEMS on the same line with 35 um slabs (cell 0.25 mm, 0.1 mm near the port, coarse 2 mm, 1.9 to 2.9 GHz): Z0 48.6 to 49.3 ohm (-1.4 to -2.8 percent). Both codes pass the closed form within 3 percent. The fitted sqrt(eps_eff) (assuming a 0.4 h end extension) is 2.02 ours and 1.92 openEMS; the closed form gives 1.83. That 5 percent was an artefact of fitting different bands and copper models (ours was a sheet, fitted over 0.5 to 3 GHz, with an assumed 0.4 h end extension). **Two-length test, same band (1.9 to 2.9 GHz), slab copper 35 um in both codes, 15 and 30 mm lines, k differenced so the end extension drops out:** ours Z0 48.5 (30 mm) and 48.9 (15 mm) ohm, eps_eff 3.04; openEMS Z0 48.6 and 49.6 ohm, eps_eff 3.09. The two codes agree on phase velocity to 0.8 percent in sqrt(eps_eff) and on Z0 to 1 ohm; both read eps_eff 9 percent below `lines.py`'s 3.33 (so the closed form or the fitted end extension, about 3.5 mm in both, is the outlier, not either solver). Line velocity and impedance are therefore ruled out as the source of the matched-geometry gap between the codes; what remains is the antenna-specific structure (the coarse region, the fine/coarse transitions around the refine boxes, the port through the inner layers, boundary distance, loss modelling). The preset does not change this check (the validation script fixes its own cells): it validates the line and the port, not the coarse-cell behaviour. Not yet run: open and short controls on the TI feed. **Status of the baseline question:** there is no independent measured baseline (TI's curve is read from a figure, see below); openEMS and our solver agree on a 50 ohm line within 3 percent and on the matched-geometry dip (`nd_*` against openEMS 0.1 mm, 2.2375 GHz) within 3 to 6 percent depending on mesh, with the dip scatter of one code alone +-1.5 percent.

*Known-load controls on the TI feed (run 2026-11-23, read 2026-11-25; `out/antennas/swrr034c/ctl/`, `ti_open.json`, `ti_short.json`, coarse preset, 0.2 mm box).* `phase_fit.py`: open: phase at f = 0 of 0.8 degrees, slope -9.4 deg/GHz, reads as open; short: 162.1 degrees (18 from 180, inside the skill's 45), slope -30.3 deg/GHz, reads as short. The port sees its load on this board. Not repeated at the medium preset; the port and feed geometry did not change since, so a rerun is not needed unless the voxelizer's snapping changes.

*CPML thickness at medium (`nd_pml24_01`, 24 cells against the default 8, matched geometry, 0.1 mm): dip -32.9 dB at 2.3746 GHz, Z 48.2+1.3j, identical to `nd_med_01` (2.3746, -32.8, 48.2+1.3j): the absorber thickness is not a factor in the coarse-cell trend below. `nd_pml4_01` and `nd_bd50_01` pending.*

*Coarse region, 60 cells per wavelength (`nd_cpw60_01`, matched geometry, 0.1 mm, 21.1 M cells, 184 336 steps, 3191 s).* Dip -17.6 dB at 2.3011 GHz, Z 61.7-9.1j. Series over the coarse cell (cells per wavelength 20, 30, 45, 60): dip 2.3746, 2.3093, 2.3419, 2.3011 GHz; R at the dip 48, 52, 56, 62 ohm; dip depth -32.8, -29.8, -24.5, -17.6 dB. R rises monotonically while the frequency scatters, so the matched geometry is not converged in R as the coarse region is refined (14 ohm over the series), and the earlier 'scatter floor' statement is withdrawn: the trend is not noise. One candidate: the CPML is a fixed 8 cells, so its physical thickness falls with the coarse cell (3 times thinner at 60 than at 20) while the boundary distance is fixed at 0.25 wavelength; queued at medium, matched, 0.1 mm: `pml_cells` 4 and 24 and `boundary_distance_wavelengths` 0.5 (`nd_pml24_01`, `nd_pml4_01`, `nd_bd50_01`). Another: the lateral growth from the refine boxes (ratio 1.1) meets a coarser cell at the box edge as cpw falls.

*Layer 3 against Dk (2026-11-25, medium, 0.1 mm, slab, decay 0.001, no bodies).* `all_nodk_med_01` (`ti_all`, with layer 3, Dk 4.5 as in TI's readme): dip -22.7 dB at 2.2930 GHz, Z 56.8-3.9j (2.30 GHz 57.7-2.0j, 2.35 GHz 63.7+9.0j). `sd_med_01` (`ti_nol3`, no layer 3, Dk 4.5): 2.2930 GHz, Z 55.1-2.7j. **The completed 2 by 2** (dip GHz / series resonance GHz, R at the series resonance ohm; medium, 0.1 mm): Dk 4.5 without layer 3 (`sd_med_01`) 2.2930 / 2.3046, 56.8; Dk 4.5 with layer 3 (`all_nodk_med_01`) 2.2930 / 2.3101, 58.7; Dk 4.2 without layer 3 (`sd_dk42_med_01`, dip -17.0 dB, Z 65.1-6.0j) 2.3256 / 2.3560, 69.8; Dk 4.2 with layer 3 (`all_nob_med_01`) 2.3909 / 2.3895, 63.8. **Layer 3** changes the dip by 0.0 percent at Dk 4.5 and by 2.8 percent at Dk 4.2, and the series resonance by 0.2 and 1.4 percent: inside the 1 to 3 percent scatter these runs show, so it is not resolved as a factor. **Dk 4.5 to 4.2** (a 6.7 percent drop) raises the series resonance by 2.2 percent (no layer 3) and 3.4 percent (with), the dip by 1.4 and 4.3 percent: about 0.3 to 0.5 percent of frequency per percent of Dk by the series resonance (the dip is shallow and flat at Dk 4.2, -17 to -18 dB, so its position is the noisier of the two metrics). R moves 6 ohm the other way between the two Dk rows without layer 3 (56.8 to 69.8), so R is not stable at the 10 ohm level either. At TI's stated Dk of 4.5 the model's dip sits 5.2 percent below TI's 2.42 GHz (read from the figure) and its series resonance 4.6 percent below; at 4.2 the dip sits 1.2 to 3.9 percent below depending on the run. So the dip frequency against TI is set by the assumed laminate permittivity, which TI gives only as 'FR4 Dk 4.5' without a frequency or a tolerance (FR4 sheets at 2.4 GHz run 4.0 to 4.5); the unexplained term is the Dk of the real board, not the solver. Pending: `sd_dk42_med_01` (no layer 3, Dk 4.2) to confirm the 2 by 2.

*Fabrication options not in the model: solder mask, surface finish, solder, copper weight (2026-11-25).* **Solder mask.** TI's readme: double-sided solder mask (`stopcomp.spl`, `stopsold.spl`). Rendering `stopcomp.spl` (negative film: drawn areas are the openings) over `l1.spl` (`out/antennas/swrr034c/mask_overlay.png`): in the inverted-F's region (x 43.5 to 71, y 121 to 132.5 mm, the 0.1 mm refine box) 9 of 46 152 copper pixels (0.02 percent) lie under an opening; the openings are at component pads and the mounting holes. **The mask covers the radiator.** (A first pass on a wrong region, a routed trace near the chip at y 113 to 122 that I took for the antenna, gave 25 percent open; the antenna is the inverted-F at the top of the board, and the box was right all along.) The model has no mask layer (the `solder_mask` entry in `fab_profiles.json`, er 3.5 and 0.01 mm, is read by nothing), so the covered radiator is an unmodelled dielectric load of known sign (lowers the resonance; the size, usually quoted as one to a few percent for 15 to 25 um of mask on a trace antenna, is not measured here and no source for it was read). A snapshot with a dielectric layer inserted above the top copper is mis-read by the stack code (the dry run assigned the top copper 12.5 um), so a mask needs a voxelizer feature: fill the cells above the top copper slab up to the mask thickness except at openings. At Dk 4.5 the model's dip is already 5 percent below TI's, so a mask term pushes the wrong way and strengthens the 4.0 to 4.2 reading of the board's effective Dk. **Surface finish (ENIG, immersion silver, HASL).** Copper is PEC, so finish enters only as thickness, and ENIG's 3 to 6 um nickel (IPC-4552) adds conductor loss (nickel conductivity is far below copper's; sources: Rogers/I-Connect007 insertion-loss article and the Sonnet ENIG note, both mostly above 2.4 GHz); an estimate of a few tenths of an ohm to about 1 ohm of series resistance on a 2.4 GHz element, a few percent of efficiency, and no effect on the resonance frequency to first order. TI's readme states no finish. Not modelled; to be recorded as a profile field so a user's choice travels with the project (no UI before discussion). **Solder joints.** Not modelled: fillet geometry and alloy conductivity (silver-bearing and lead-free solders are about eight times more resistive than copper, estimate; skin depth about 4 um); silver terminations dissolve into molten solder (silver leaching), which is why silver-bearing solders are used on them, a reliability effect and not an RF one. Irrelevant to the TI printed antenna, possibly relevant to a W3008 whose internals are unknown anyway. **Copper weight.** PCBWay offers 1 oz to 8 oz (35 to 280 um) on the outer layers and 1 to 4 oz on the inner layers (capabilities page read via search snippets, not the page itself; its help page says up to 13 oz, a discrepancy); inner layers are finished at the foil weight (no plating), outer layers gain copper from plating, so '1 oz outer' is about 55 to 65 um finished (third-party figure). Our slabs are 35 um on all layers (TI's readme says '0.35um Cu PER LAYER', read as a typo for 35 um). Runs `nd_cu70_01` (70 um all layers) and `nd_cu17_01` (17.5 um) queued against `nd_med_01` at medium, 0.1 mm, Dk 4.5.

*Solder mask bound (queued 2026-11-25; `ti_nd_mask.json`, matched geometry, medium, 0.1 mm, Dk 4.5).* No code change: a pseudo-component `MASK` (F.Cu side, outline x 43.5 to 71, y 121 to 132.5 mm, height 0.06 mm) uses the existing dielectric-body feature, which fills air cells only; a dry run shows 63 250 cells, two cell layers over the antenna region, so about 80 um of dielectric over the copper and 35 um beside it (the first two z cells above the board: copper slab height, then the next air cell). That is 3 times the real mask over copper (10 to 25 um), so eps 3.5 (`nd_mask35_01`) is an upper bound on the mask's effect and eps 1.8 (`nd_mask18_01`, about the dielectric volume of a 25 um layer) an estimate; both vs `nd_med_01` (2.3746 GHz, R 48 ohm).

*Sensitivity runs, results (2026-11-25; matched geometry `ti_nol3_nodrop.json` unless stated, medium, 0.1 mm antenna box, slab copper, Dk 4.5; baseline `nd_med_01` dip 2.3746 GHz, -32.8 dB, Z 48.2+1.3j).* **The dip is read on the 8.2 MHz frequency grid (0.34 % of 2.4 GHz), so every shift below 8 MHz is at or under one bin and cannot be sized from these runs; only the series resonance or a sub-bin fit can.** Dip frequency, depth, Z at the dip: dielectric thickness +10 % (`nd_thp10_01`) 2.3664 GHz (-1 bin), -53.1 dB, 49.8+0.1j; -10 % (`nd_thm10_01`) 2.3827 GHz (+1 bin), -39.6 dB, 49.9+1.0j; copper 17.5 um (`nd_cu17_01`, 0.0494 ps step, 1553 s) 2.3746 GHz (0), -37.5 dB, 48.9+0.6j; copper 70 um (`nd_cu70_01`) 2.3746 GHz (0), -28.6 dB, 47.4+2.5j; mask eps 1.8 (`nd_mask18_01`) 2.3664 GHz (-1 bin), -30.4 dB, 47.4+1.4j; mask eps 3.5 (`nd_mask35_01`, upper bound, mask about 3 times too thick) 2.3501 GHz (-3 bins, -1.0 %), -27.6 dB, 46.0+0.1j; boundary distance 0.5 wavelength (`nd_bd50_01`) 2.3093 GHz (-8 bins, -2.8 %), -20.5 dB, 59.9-3.0j. Reading: (1) thickness +-10 % moves the dip by one bin in the expected direction (thicker substrate, lower frequency), so the sensitivity is at most about 0.3 % per 10 %, consistent with a series-resonance shift of that order but unsized here; (2) copper weight does not move the dip within one bin; the depth moves monotonically with copper thickness (-37.5, -32.8, -28.6 dB for 17.5, 35, 70 um) but depth is the noisy metric when the dip is deep, so it is not claimed; (3) the mask bound is -1.0 % (dip bin) at 3 times the real thickness and eps 3.5, so the real mask is at most a few tenths of a percent (about -0.3 % if linear in thickness, an estimate); the estimate case (1.8) is one bin; (4) the boundary distance is the only change that moves the dip beyond 1 %, 2.8 %, and it moves R from 48 to 60 ohm: this is a solver or mesh convergence issue, not a physical term, and it is not yet explained (CPML thickness is ruled out; `nd_g105_01` and `nd_g12_01` test the lateral growth ratio). **Sub-bin re-read (`scripts/antenna/subbin.py`: reactance zero crossing by linear interpolation, and a parabola through the three bins around the dip; the two agree within about 2 MHz except the boundary run).** Baseline `nd_med_01`: series resonance 2.3714 GHz, parabola 2.3733. Shift of the series resonance against the baseline (MHz, %): thickness +10 % -5.3 (-0.22 %), -10 % +8.5 (+0.36 %); copper 17.5 um +1.7 (+0.07 %), 70 um -3.2 (-0.13 %); mask eps 1.8 -8.4 (-0.35 %), eps 3.5 at 3 times the real thickness -21.4 (-0.90 %); CPML 4 cells -7.9 (-0.33 %), CPML 24 cells +0.1; boundary 0.5 wavelength -47 (-2.0 %, the series resonance is poorly defined there, the dip bin says -2.8 %). Reading: thickness about -0.03 % per % (both signs agree in direction, the two sides differ by 3 MHz, which is the confound below); copper weight about 0.1 % per doubling and monotonic (thicker, lower); the solder mask is an unsized -0.3 % at most (the 3.5 bound scaled to the real thickness gives -0.3 %, the eps 1.8 case -0.35 %), so it points toward lower frequency (about 7 MHz) but this is a direction, not a size, until the eps 1.0 grid control (`nd_mask10_01`) separates the grid part. **Confound common to every row (corrected after the advisor review):** each variation also changes the grid, and the only direct measurement of a grid-only change (bodies added, medium preset) moved the dip by about +1 %. So the grid part can be up to about 1 %, which is larger than every row except the boundary distance: thickness, copper weight and the eps 1.8 mask give a **direction, not a size**, until the grid part is separated. `nd_cu70_01` changed the time step from 0.0776 to 0.0975 ps (a large z-grid change), so its -0.13 % is unsized. **CPML:** the 4, 8 and 24 cell runs do not share a grid either (grid 419 x 273 x 71, 426 x 281 x 79 and 458 x 313 x 111 cells; time step 0.0772, 0.0776 and 0.0775 ps), so the -0.33 % of the 4-cell run is the absorber plus a small grid change, not a clean absorber error; what the three runs do show is that 8 and 24 cells agree to 0.1 MHz in the series resonance, so 8 is converged. There is no run-to-run scatter at a fixed grid (the solver is deterministic); the "scatter" is the grid dependence. **Boundary distance, two readings (2026-11-25, written before the runs land):** (A) 0.25 wavelength is too close, the near field reaches the absorber, and 0.5 wavelength (2.3093 GHz, R 60) is nearer the truth, which would move the baseline by 2 to 3 %, the same size as the Dk gap to TI; the R 48 to 60 shift matching the `cpw60` shift (R 48 to 62) supports "more air converges to R about 60"; (B) the larger box brings a solver problem (a slower resonance of the air region, mesh transition). `nd_bd75_01` (0.75 wavelength) decides: if it sits at the 0.5 value within about 0.5 %, reading A holds and 0.5 wavelength becomes the baseline for the ledger; if it keeps drifting, reading B holds and that is the solver finding. `nd_mask10_01` (the MASK body with eps 1.0, no physics) gives the grid part of the mask runs: the mask term is the eps 1.8 shift minus the eps 1.0 shift. `nd_g105_01` and `nd_g12_01` test the lateral growth ratio. Both run behind the antenna queue.

**Farm placement is already by speed (2026-11-25).** When the Mac became a worker the first Dk job went to it while the RTX was busy; that is correct, not a placement fault: `Scheduler._idle_slots` sorts the idle devices by measured Mcells/s (fastest first, unmeasured last, then worker name) and a job is never held back for a busy faster device. Three scenarios pin the order. Open question, not built: holding a long job for a much faster busy device (needs a job-length estimate).

**The rest of the 0.75 wavelength set, and the decay runs (2026-11-25).** Baseline `nd_bd75_01` series resonance 2.4005 GHz. `fd_thp10` (dielectric thicknesses +10 %; 92 832 steps, 1189 s, 1586 Mcells/s): series resonance 2.3798 GHz, **-0.86 %**, dip 2.3827 GHz, -18.2 dB (predicted -0.2 to -0.4 %: right sign, above the range, inside the +-1 % grid-noise rule). `fd_mask18` (MASK body eps 1.8; 120 032 steps, 1462 s, 1668 Mcells/s): 2.3854 GHz, **-0.63 %**, dip 2.3827 GHz, -22.6 dB (predicted -0.2 to -0.35 %: same reading). Thickness is therefore +0.59 % for -10 % and -0.86 % for +10 %: opposite signs as predicted, roughly 0.07 %/% and the sizes are 2 to 3 times the 0.25 box prediction (0.2 to 0.4 %), so thickness and mask are sized at the 0.5 to 1 % level with an uncertainty as large as the term (grid change about 1 %); they stay 'consistent with small, not a measurement'. Dk stays the one term that is large and, at this box, of the opposite sign to the 0.25 box. **Decay (Mac, MoltenVK):** `nd_dec00003_01` (decay 3e-4; 182 672 steps) series resonance 2.3667 GHz and `nd_dec00001_01` (decay 1e-4; 195 360 steps) 2.3669 GHz against 2.3714 GHz at decay 1e-3 (`nd_med_01`, 117 952 steps): **-0.20 %**, identical to each other, dip -33.2 and -33.6 dB at 2.3664 GHz. Same box as `nd_med_01` (0.25 wavelength, default), so the term is clean and, being a truncation in time that does not change the board's electrical length, it should transfer to other boxes (not yet measured there). The stop criterion is converged at 3e-4 to better than 0.02 % and the default (1e-3) costs about 0.2 %; the ledger term is -0.2 % +- 0.05 %, removable by running at 3e-4. Farm: all four RTX jobs ran to completion with no thermal or driver events (max 53 C, 97 W, reported by the Windows session) and the turn was released each time.

**First two results of the 0.75 wavelength sensitivity set, run on the RTX through the farm (2026-11-25): one prediction holds, one is falsified.** Baseline `nd_bd75_01`: series resonance 2.4005 GHz, R 59 ohm, depth -21.4 dB. `fd_thm10` (all three dielectric thicknesses -10 %; 94 192 steps, 1143 s, 1675 Mcells/s): series resonance 2.4146 GHz (**+0.59 %**, predicted +0.2 to +0.4 %: right sign, slightly above the range), dip 2.4072 GHz, -24.2 dB, Z 56.2-2.1j. `fd_dk42` (all three dielectrics Dk 4.5 to 4.2; 128 224 steps, 1395 s, 1810 Mcells/s): series resonance 2.3294 GHz, **-2.96 %**, dip 2.3174 GHz, -21.6 dB, Z 58.5-3.1j. **The prediction (+2.0 to +3.3 %, the sign of the 0.25 wavelength box) is falsified: lowering Dk moved the resonance down by 3 %**, 5 to 6 % from what the dielectric alone should do. The grid is the same size class (474 x 327 x 127, time step 0.0775 ps against 0.0774) and the settings did apply (the run's settings.json shows 4.2 on all three layers). Reading: the domain-size term found at fixed Dk (+-1.6 %, oscillating with the box) is not a fixed offset: changing the board's own electrical length moves the phase of that interference, so a difference taken at one box size mixes the physical effect with a different point on the domain-size oscillation. Consequences: (1) sensitivities measured at one box size cannot be carried to another, and the earlier 0.25 wavelength figures (Dk, thickness, copper, mask) are not transferable either; (2) the largest solver term in the ledger is the open-boundary / domain-size dependence, now known to be able to exceed the Dk effect it competes with; (3) the physically controlled way to get a Dk sensitivity is a sweep of Dk at each of several box sizes and a mean over the oscillation (or a converged absorbing boundary that removes it), which the farm can run. `fd_thp10` and `fd_mask18` are still to come; the advisor is called when they and the two decay runs are in.

**`nd_bd100_01` landed (2026-11-25): the box-size dependence is an oscillation, not a convergence.** 1.0 wavelength box (grid 501 x 355 x 155 = 27.6 M cells, 129 840 steps, 2942 s, MoltenVK): series resonance **2.3725 GHz**, R at the dip 60.3 ohm, dip -18.2 dB at 2.2603 GHz (a shallow, flat dip: the reactance crosses zero at 2.3725 GHz, so the series resonance is the number to use). Against the prediction written beforehand: "if the resonance returns toward 2.37 GHz the dependence repeats about every half wavelength (a standing wave off the boundary); if it sits near 2.40 GHz, 0.75 wavelength is converged and 0.5 was the outlier". It returned to 2.37 GHz. Series resonance against boundary distance: 0.25 wavelength 2.3714 GHz, 0.5 wavelength 2.3241 GHz (-2.0 %), 0.75 wavelength 2.4005 GHz (+1.2 %), 1.0 wavelength 2.3725 GHz (+0.05 %): mean 2.367 GHz, swing +-1.6 % about it, no trend to converge to. With the 16-cell CPML result (the absorber is not what moves it) the remaining mechanism is the finite domain interfering with the board's radiated and edge waves, with an amplitude that does not shrink between 0.5 and 1.0 wavelength. R is steady at about 60 ohm for every box of 0.5 wavelength or more (only the 0.25 default gives 48 ohm). **Ledger term from this:** domain-size dependence of the series resonance, +-1.6 % peak (about 1.2 % rms), sign unpredictable, mechanism open; the baseline 2.3714 GHz happens to sit at the centre of the swing, and the default 0.25 wavelength box is therefore not worse than the larger ones for frequency but gives the wrong R (48 against 60 ohm) and depth. The 0.75 wavelength sensitivity set now running on the RTX sits at the top of the swing (2.4005 GHz): its differences are deltas at one box size and carry the same +-1 % grid noise as before.

**`nd_bd50_pml16_01` landed (2026-11-25): the CPML thickness is not the cause of the box-size dependence.** 0.5 wavelength box with 16 CPML cells (grid 467 x 322 x 121 = 18.2 M cells, 115 328 steps, 2182 s, MoltenVK): series resonance 2.3261 GHz against 2.3241 GHz with 8 cells (+0.09 %), dip 2.3174 GHz against 2.3093 GHz (+0.35 %, one bin), R at the dip 59.7 against 59.9 ohm, depth -20.9 against -20.5 dB. By the prediction written beforehand ("within 0.5 %: the box itself is resonant, not absorber reflection") the absorber at grazing incidence is not what moves the resonance by 2 % between 0.25 and 0.5 wavelength: doubling the CPML changes it by 0.1 %. What remains is the domain itself (the board's near field and the ground plane's edge waves interacting with the box size) or the mesh in the larger domain; `nd_bd100_01` is the next test.

**Farm status addendum (later on 2026-11-25).** *Built since the status paragraph at the top of this section:* coordinator hardening (the queue is saved to `jobs.json` and restored after a restart, a running job is requeued with its attempt counted, `gates.json` is re-read at every dispatch, `serve` stops cleanly on SIGTERM and gives back a held turn); the worker agent lifecycle (`agent configure|start|stop|restart|status|logs|doctor|autostart`, `setup`, `worker --from-agent`, `agent.status.json`) in `farm/agent.py` and `farm/autostart.py`; a desktop window for it (`emfdtd-farm gui`, tkinter only, no port); a self-contained worker bundle (`scripts/package_farm_agent.sh`, the launchers in `plugin/tools/`, `docs/farm-setup.md`), verified by unpacking it and running it alone with a clean interpreter; lock-command workers announce their GPUs from a saved list (`devices --save`) and every call that waits for the lock has a multi-hour allowance, so a long run holding the shared lock does not stop a worker from joining; a CI failure (a thread started before the members it reads existed) fixed and verified under ASan with 10 repeated runs and under CPU load. *Not yet deployed to the running coordinator* (it holds a Windows turn until the RTX batch ends). *Enrolled but not started:* the Mac as `worker:mac-m1` (waits for its one-time device recording in the Mac GPU lock queue). *Still open:* the Farm tab's Fetch-and-open and "Where a run goes" (an agent is on it), Windows-side tests of the new agent commands and the launcher, a Windows build of the window.

**Idea from the Windows side, not built: prepare before the turn.** On the board job the coordinator held the Windows GPU turn for 737 s of which about 230 s (31 %) were CPU-only model preparation (the GPU idle at 5 %). The solver creates its Vulkan device only after the model is prepared (`main.cpp`: `prepared`, then `Context`), so a flag such as `--wait-for-go` could stop right there: print `ready`, wait for a `go` line on stdin, then create the device. A coordinator-held-turn worker would start the solver with no turn, send `prepared` when it prints `ready`, and the coordinator would then take the turn and answer `go` (the offer would carry `turn: false` until then). It works only where the coordinator holds the turn (a worker's own lock command wraps the whole process); the cost is a protocol step, a stdin pipe on the worker's child and tests for cancel and partition during preparation. Worth about 30 % less shared-GPU time per large job; do it when the farm is used a lot.

**Sensitivity set at the 0.75 wavelength box, sent to the RTX through the farm (2026-11-25; predictions written before the runs).** Why: every sensitivity so far (thickness, copper, mask, Dk) was measured in the 0.25 wavelength box, which is not converged (+-1.5 % in domain size). Baseline: `nd_bd75_01` (Mac, MoltenVK): series resonance 2.4005 GHz, dip 2.3991 GHz, R 59.3 ohm, 20.3 M cells, 93 568 steps; an RTX run equals a Mac run to 4e-7, so the baseline is not repeated. Jobs (matched geometry `ti_nol3_nodrop.json`, medium preset, 0.1 mm antenna box, slab copper, `boundary_distance_wavelengths=0.75`, everything else as `nd_med_01`): `fd_dk42` (all three dielectrics Dk 4.2), `fd_thm10` and `fd_thp10` (dielectric thicknesses -10 % and +10 %), `fd_mask18` (the MASK body, eps 1.8). Predictions from the 0.25 box and from the Dk series (resonance moves about 0.3 to 0.5 % per 1 % of Dk): Dk 4.5 to 4.2 (-6.7 %) raises the resonance by 2.0 to 3.3 % (to about 2.45 to 2.48 GHz); thickness +-10 % moves it by 0.2 to 0.4 % in the opposite direction; the mask (eps 1.8) lowers it by 0.2 to 0.35 %. Grid changes add up to about 1 % of noise to each difference, so a result inside +-1 % of the prediction for the three small terms is "consistent", not a measurement; the Dk term is far larger than that. If the thickness and mask terms are again within one bin they stay unsized and the ledger carries them as bounds.

**Machine-readable CLI (2026-11-25, "the cheaper step" instead of an MCP server).** `emfdtd --json` prints one JSON document (schema 1, additive changes only; `docs/cli-json.md`) for `--list-settings` (typed values, defaults, ranges, choices), `--list-devices` (the index `--device` accepts, memory, Vulkan version, usable), `--dry-run` (plan, estimate, changed settings, notes), a headless run (results folder, steps, wall time, Mcells/s, best match, exit status) and the new `--run-info RUNDIR [--s11]` (a saved run summarized from `summary.txt`, `settings.json` and `s11.s1p`: grid, steps, best match, the -10 dB band around it, optionally the S11 table). In `--json` mode file descriptor 1 is pointed at stderr and the document goes to a copy of the real stdout, so every existing human line stays byte-identical and stdout is exactly one document; failures are `{"error": ...}` with the usual exit code. The builders are in `src/app/cli_json.*` (tested without Vulkan: `tests/cpu/cli_json_test.cpp`, and the binary by `scripts/cli_json_check.sh` in `ctest -L cpu`). Why not an MCP server yet: the CLI plus `--json` gives an assistant a stable interface without a new process or a new way past the farm's confirmations; an MCP wrapper (read-only tools first; no enable/enroll/revoke) can be built on it later. `--json` is refused with the window, `--view`, `--sweep` and `--prune-runs`. Measured: the sanitizer build is not suited to a CPU run (a 7 328-step coarse test board took 32 s in release, over three minutes under ASan/UBSan).

**Dk 4.2 at the 0.5 wavelength box (2026-11-25, `fd_dk42_bd05`, RTX through the farm; 106 400 steps, 899 s, 1668 Mcells/s).** Series resonance 2.3322 GHz against 2.3241 GHz for Dk 4.5 at the same box: **+0.35 %** (dip 2.3093 GHz, -19.8 dB). Dk 4.5 to 4.2 now reads about +2 to +3 % at 0.25, **+0.35 % at 0.5**, **-2.96 % at 0.75**, and **-1.10 % at 1.0** (`fd_dk42_bd10b`: series resonance 2.3464 GHz against 2.3725; 114 576 steps, 2493 s on the Mac at 1213 Mcells/s; dip 2.3338 GHz, -21.8 dB; the first attempt `fd_dk42_bd10` was stopped by the worker's 45 minute ceiling): not monotonic, a scatter of about +-3 % with the box size, the same kind of scatter the known-answer cavity shows from the grid (see the known-answer paragraph), so the box-size term is not independent of Dk and a single-box Dk sensitivity cannot be quoted. This is what the advisor's discriminating test was for: it says the mean of the Dk 4.2 series will not sit +2 to +3 % above the Dk 4.5 mean, so the 'phase of the interference' reading is not enough, and something in how the domain size interacts with the permittivity (the mesh, the CPML distance in cells, the lumped port reference) is the next suspect. The first attempt at `fd_dk42_bd10` ran 45 minutes on the Mac and was stopped by the worker's 45 minute ceiling (about 65 minutes were needed): it is resubmitted when the estimate and pinning work lands.

**Overnight queue 2026-11-26 (user asked for everything that blocks trust in the model and solver to be worked on while they sleep; farm jobs on the RTX and the Mac are authorized for this validation work).** What blocks trust, in order: (1) the domain-size term, about +-1.6 % on the series resonance, unexplained; (2) the Dk response changing sign with the box size (+2 to +3 % at 0.25 wavelengths, +0.35 % at 0.5, -2.96 % at 0.75, 1.0 pending), which a correct solver cannot do; (3) the reactance slope near resonance (about 13 ohm per 50 MHz here against about 31 for openEMS in an earlier run): a factor of two in Q; (4) no measurement to compare with (the mini VNA is not set up: outside the solver). Work items: **A** a known-answer case (a structure with an analytic or reference answer) run over box size and Dk to separate solver error from board-specific effects (agent H; results in `out/validation/`, a paragraph here); **B** a like-for-like openEMS re-check of the slope with a bisection if it persists (agent I; no installs); **C** the board itself at larger boxes (1.5 wavelengths, 2.0 if it fits) to see whether the box term ever settles (needs the Mac worker's ceiling raised after `fd_dk42_bd10b` ends, and a coordinator restart while nothing runs); **D** the error ledger with sizes and uncertainties and the trusted band (written after A to C, advisor consulted); **E** the checkpoint work continues (solver side in progress, farm half next). Rules for the night: no installs, no deletions, no changes to KiCad files, GPU only through the locks, farm jobs only with the estimate-aware scheduler, never restart the coordinator or the Mac agent while a job runs (a restart requeues it from step 0), keep the Windows worker's 45 minute ceiling (jobs that need more go to the Mac), commit finished and tested work in small commits with the check passing.
**Known-answer study, 2026-11-26 (`scripts/antenna/known_answer.py`, results in `out/validation/`, all runs on the farm): the domain-size term and the Dk sign flip are mesh and rasterization errors, not physics.** Structures: a probe-fed 2.45 GHz patch on 1.6 mm FR-4 (geometry fixed from the cavity-model design at Dk 4.5; cavity-model resonance 2.4500 GHz at Dk 4.5 and 2.5328 GHz at Dk 4.2, expected shift +3.38 %) and a closed cavity (45 x 30 mm, via-wall ring, two copper planes: exact TM110 2.8826 GHz at Dk 4.5, shift +3.51 %, independent of the outer box). Band 2.0 to 2.6 GHz (patch) and 2.4 to 3.3 GHz (cavity), 601 points; resonance = parabola through the |S11| minimum. *Default mesh (medium, 20 cells per wavelength, no refinement):* patch f(Dk 4.5) = 2.2364, 2.2241, 2.2866, 2.2866, 2.2242, 2.1548 GHz for boxes 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0 wavelengths (a 6 % spread; the values repeat exactly: discrete states), Dk shift +2.94, +4.03, +3.49, +3.54, +2.85, +3.94 % (expected +3.38). The closed cavity, which no outer boundary can touch, gives 2.8978, 2.9144, 2.9162, 2.9224, 2.8873, 2.8935 GHz (a 1.2 % spread) and shifts +3.43, +4.04, +3.15, +2.88, +4.01, +4.24 % (exact +3.51). *Cause, shown by three experiments:* (1) the dumped grid (`out/validation/dumpgrid.cpp`, scratch) puts the patch's radiating edges on cells of 2.3 to 2.6 mm that change with the box, and the TI board's coarse cell varies from 2.5 to 3.4 mm with the box; (2) translating the whole board by 0.05 to 0.25 mm leaves the grid identical relative to the copper but moves f(Dk 4.2, box 0.25) from 2.3021 to 2.3660 GHz (+2.8 %) at 0.05 and 0.10 mm; (3) moving only the patch copper by -2 nm in x and y (nothing physical) gives f(Dk 4.2) 2.3669 instead of 2.3021 GHz, and +5 nm in y gives 2.3089. The sheet-copper test in `voxelize.cpp` (`insideShape`, a strict even-odd ray cast) is evaluated exactly on a polygon edge because snapping puts a grid line on every simple copper corner; the result is decided by rounding, so a whole row or column of PEC edges along a patch edge is in or out at random (the comment above `insideShapeOrOn` describes the same hazard and fixes it for slab layers only). *With the structure resolved the errors disappear:* patch with a refinement box around it (cell 0.8, 0.5, 0.3 mm), boxes 0.25 to 1.0: f(4.5) = 2.3287/2.3253/2.3455/2.3457, 2.3457/2.3445/2.3571/2.3569, 2.3545/2.3540/2.3631/2.3631 GHz (converging, box spread 0.7 %, 0.5 %, 0.4 %), Dk shift +3.29 to +3.41 %, +3.33 to +3.40 %, +3.34 to +3.42 % (expected +3.38): constant to 0.1 %.
